Formalizes the four classical energy conditions of GR\n10\tas predicates on an abstract stress-energy bilinear form, with chain\n11\timplications and explicit counterexample witnesses (cosmological-\n12\tconstant fluid violates SEC; stiff fluid violates DEC; ghost scalar\n13\tviolates NEC).\n14\t\n15\tPer the Phase 6f deep-research audit (`Lit-Search/Phase-6f/Phase 6f\n16\taudit — Classical GR Lean infrastructure.md` §3E + §5.6f.3): no proof\n17\tassistant has formalized these four predicates with chain\n18\timplications + counterexample witnesses. PhysLean has neither;\n19\tMathlib has neither. **First formalization in any proof assistant.**\n20\t\n21\t## Scoping mode\n22\t\n23\tThis module ships the **abstract bilinear form** version of the\n24\tenergy conditions: a stress-energy tensor `T : V → V → ℝ` on a 4D\n25\treal inner-product-like vector space `V`, parameterized by a metric\n26\tsignature `g : V → V → ℝ`. The full Lorentzian-manifold version\n27\t(stress-energy tensor field on a globally hyperbolic spacetime)\n28\trequires the Phase 6f.4 Lorentzian-metric infrastructure and is\n29\tdeferred until that lands.\n30\t\n31\tThe abstract version captures all the algebraic content needed for\n32\tPhase 6a Wave 6 (`PositiveMassTheorem.lean`, Witten's spinor proof),\n33\twhich uses DEC of `T_μν^emerg` at a point-wise level — no global\n34\tspacetime structure needed at the predicate level.\n35\t\n36\t## Energy conditions (Hawking-Ellis 1973 §4.3 conventions)\n37\t\n38\tWe work in signature `−+++` throughout. Future-directed timelike means\n39\t`g(v, v) < 0` AND time-component positive (encoded as a separate\n40\tpredicate `IsFutureDirectedTimelike`).\n41\t\n42\tFor a stress-energy tensor `T : V → V → ℝ` and a metric `g : V → V → ℝ`:\n43\t\n44\t- **NEC (Null Energy Condition):** for every null vector `k`\n45\t  (`g(k, k) = 0`, `k ≠ 0`), `T(k, k) ≥ 0`.\n46\t- **WEC (Weak Energy Condition):** for every future-directed timelike\n47\t  vector `v`, `T(v, v) ≥ 0`.\n48\t- **DEC (Dominant Energy Condition):** for every future-directed\n49\t  timelike `v`, `T(v, v) ≥ 0` AND the vector `T^μ{}_ν v^ν` (the\n50\t  energy-flux 4-vector) is future-directed causal. We encode DEC as\n51\t  WEC + a 4-flux causality hypothesis on `T(v, ·)`.\n52\t- **SEC (Strong Energy Condition):** for every future-directed\n53\t  timelike `v`, `(T - (1/2)·trace(T)·g)(v, v) ≥ 0`. Equivalently,\n54\t  the trace-reversed stress-energy satisfies WEC.\n55\t\n56\t## Chain implications and counterexamples\n57\t\n58\tThe classical chain implications (Wald §9.2, Hawking-Ellis §4.3):\n59\t\n60\t- DEC ⇒ WEC ⇒ NEC (provable from definitions, modulo continuity in\n61\t  the WEC ⇒ NEC step).\n62\t- SEC ⇏ WEC, WEC ⇏ SEC: counterexample is the cosmological-Λ fluid\n63\t  (`ρ = -p = Λ/(8π) > 0` satisfies WEC, NEC, DEC but VIOLATES SEC\n64\t  for `Λ > 0`).\n65\t\n66\tThis module ships explicit counterexample witnesses for the\n67\tnon-implications.\n68\t\n69\t## Anti-pattern audit (4-pattern check)\n70\t\n71\tPer the audit's §5.6f.3 P1/P3/P4 checklist:\n72\t\n73\t1. **No ∃-absorption (P1):** counterexample witnesses are explicit\n74\t   tensors (cosmological-Λ, stiff-fluid, ghost-scalar), not\n75\t   `∃ T, P(T)` discharged by a black box.\n76\t2. **No P3 (trivial-multiplication-as-physics):** WEC is stated for\n77\t   *non-zero* timelike vectors, so the trivial-`v=0` case is excluded\n78\t   by hypothesis.\n79\t3. **No P4 (vacuous axioms):** WEC is NOT stated as\n80\t   `∀ v, T(v,v) ≥ 0` (which would be equivalent to T being\n81\t   positive-semi-definite, false in any non-trivial Lorentzian\n82\t   theory). The timelike restriction is load-bearing.\n83\t\n84\t## References\n85\t\n86\t- R.M. Wald, *General Relativity* (1984) §9.2.\n87\t- S.W. Hawking, G.F.R. Ellis, *The Large Scale Structure of Space-\n88\t  Time* (1973) §4.3.\n89\t- S. Carroll, *Spacetime and Geometry* (2004) §4.6.\n90\t\n91\t## Cross-system landscape\n92\t\n93\tPer the Phase 6f audit §3, no proof assistant — Lean (Mathlib4 + Bonn\n94\t+ PhysLean), Coq, Isabelle/AFP (Smooth_Manifolds, Lie_Groups,\n95\tSchutz_Spacetime), HOL Light, HOL4, Mizar, Agda — has formalized\n96\tthese four predicates with chain implications and counterexamples.\n97\tThis is plausibly the first such formalization.\n98\t-/\n99\t\n100\tnamespace SKEFTHawking.EnergyConditions\n101\t\n102\topen Real\n103\t\n104\t/-! ## §1 — Abstract carrier types -/\n105\t\n106\t/--\n107\t**4-dimensional real vector space carrier.** We use `Fin 4 → ℝ`\n108\tthroughout for concreteness; the predicate definitions below\n109\tgeneralize to any 4-dimensional real vector space, but the explicit\n110\twitness construction (§5) is easier with a concrete index set.\n111\t-/\n112\tabbrev Vec4 : Type := Fin 4 → ℝ\n113\t\n114\t/--\n115\t**Stress-energy tensor as a (0,2) symmetric bilinear form.**\n116\tEncoded as a function `T : Vec4 → Vec4 → ℝ` with symmetry hypothesis\n117\tcarried alongside as a Prop bundle.\n118\t-/\n119\tabbrev StressEnergyTensor : Type := Vec4 → Vec4 → ℝ\n120\t\n121\t/--\n122\t**Metric tensor as a (0,2) symmetric bilinear form.** Same encoding\n123\tas `StressEnergyTensor`; signature distinction (Lorentzian vs.\n124\tRiemannian) is encoded via an external predicate `IsLorentzian`.\n125\t-/\n126\tabbrev MetricTensor : Type := Vec4 → Vec4 → ℝ\n127\t\n128\t/-! ## §2 — Causal-vector predicates (signature `−+++`) -/\n129\t\n130\t/--\n131\t**Null vector** w.r.t. metric `g`: `g(k, k) = 0` AND `k ≠ 0`.\n132\tThe non-zero requirement is load-bearing — the zero vector is null\n133\tin any signature, but is not a physical null direction.\n134\t-/\n135\tdef IsNull (g : MetricTensor) (k : Vec4) : Prop :=\n136\t  g k k = 0 ∧ k ≠ 0\n137\t\n138\t/--\n139\t**Timelike vector** w.r.t. metric `g`: `g(v, v) < 0` (in signature\n140\t`−+++`).\n141\t-/\n142\tdef IsTimelike (g : MetricTensor) (v : Vec4) : Prop :=\n143\t  g v v < 0\n144\t\n145\t/--\n146\t**Future-directed timelike** w.r.t. metric `g` and a chosen\n147\tfuture-pointing time-direction `t : Vec4`: timelike AND\n148\t`g(t, v) < 0`.\n149\t\n150\tThe sign convention follows signature `−+++`: for `t = v = (1,0,0,0)`,\n151\t`g(t, v) = -1 < 0`, which marks `v` as future-directed when `t` is\n152\tfuture-directed. The time-direction parameter `t` is supplied\n153\texternally so the definition is signature-agnostic and\n154\torientation-explicit (no implicit choice of \"the\" time direction).\n155\t-/\n156\tdef IsFutureDirectedTimelike (g : MetricTensor) (t v : Vec4) : Prop :=\n157\t  IsTimelike g v ∧ g t v < 0\n158\t\n159\t/-! ## §3 — Energy condition predicates -/\n160\t\n161\t/--\n162\t**Null Energy Condition (NEC).** For every null vector `k`,\n163\t`T(k, k) ≥ 0`. Hawking-Ellis 1973 §4.3.\n164\t\n165\tAnti-pattern audit: the `IsNull g k` hypothesis is load-bearing —\n166\twithout it, the predicate would also constrain the zero vector,\n167\twhich is vacuous (`T(0, 0) = 0` always). With it, the predicate is\n168\tnon-trivial: a ghost scalar field violates NEC at every non-zero\n169\tnull `k`.\n170\t-/\n171\tdef NEC (T : StressEnergyTensor) (g : MetricTensor) : Prop :=\n172\t  ∀ k : Vec4, IsNull g k → 0 ≤ T k k\n173\t\n174\t/--\n175\t**Weak Energy Condition (WEC).** For every future-directed timelike\n176\tvector `v`, `T(v, v) ≥ 0`. Hawking-Ellis 1973 §4.3.\n177\t\n178\tAnti-pattern audit: the `IsFutureDirectedTimelike g t v` hypothesis\n179\tis load-bearing — without the timelike restriction the predicate\n180\tbecomes positive-semi-definiteness of `T`, which fails on any\n181\tnon-trivial Lorentzian theory.\n182\t-/\n183\tdef WEC (T : StressEnergyTensor) (g : MetricTensor) (t : Vec4) : Prop :=\n184\t  ∀ v : Vec4, IsFutureDirectedTimelike g t v → 0 ≤ T v v\n185\t\n186\t/--\n187\t**Dominant Energy Condition (DEC).** WEC PLUS the additional\n188\tconstraint that the energy-flux 4-vector `T(v, ·)` is itself\n189\tfuture-directed causal for every future-directed timelike `v`.\n190\t\n191\tFollowing Hawking-Ellis 1973 §4.3.2, we encode the causal-flux\n192\tconstraint via the bilinear pairing `T(v, w)` for a second\n193\tfuture-directed timelike `w`: DEC requires `T(v, w) ≥ 0` whenever\n194\tboth `v` and `w` are future-directed timelike. (This is equivalent\n195\tto the `T^μ{}_ν v^ν` future-directed-causal formulation at the\n196\tpredicate level when `T` is symmetric.)\n197\t-/\n198\tdef DEC (T : StressEnergyTensor) (g : MetricTensor) (t : Vec4) : Prop :=\n199\t  WEC T g t ∧\n200\t    ∀ v w : Vec4,\n201\t      IsFutureDirectedTimelike g t v → IsFutureDirectedTimelike g t w →\n202\t      0 ≤ T v w\n203\t\n204\t/--\n205\t**Strong Energy Condition (SEC).** For every future-directed\n206\ttimelike `v`, `(T - (1/2)·tr(T)·g)(v, v) ≥ 0`. Equivalently, the\n207\ttrace-reversed stress-energy satisfies WEC.\n208\t\n209\tWe supply the trace `trT : ℝ` as an external real parameter (the\n210\ttrace requires raising an index with `g⁻¹`, which we don't yet\n211\thave in this abstract carrier). The SEC predicate then asserts:\n212\t\n213\t    ∀ v fdtl, T(v, v) - (1/2) trT · g(v, v) ≥ 0.\n214\t\n215\tIn signature `−+++`, future-directed timelike `v` has `g(v, v) < 0`,\n216\tso the `g(v, v)` term contributes positively when `trT > 0` and\n217\tnegatively when `trT < 0`. For a cosmological-constant fluid\n218\t(`ρ = -p = Λ/(8π)`, `trT = ρ + 3p · (-1) = ... `), we'll see explicitly\n219\twhich way SEC fails.\n220\t-/\n221\tdef SEC (T : StressEnergyTensor) (g : MetricTensor) (t : Vec4) (trT : ℝ) : Prop :=\n222\t  ∀ v : Vec4, IsFutureDirectedTimelike g t v →\n223\t    0 ≤ T v v - (1/2) * trT * g v v\n224\t\n225\t/-! ## §4 — Chain implications\n226\t\n227\tThe classical chain DEC ⇒ WEC ⇒ NEC. The first implication is\n228\tdirect from the DEC definition (DEC = WEC ∧ extra). The second\n229\t(WEC ⇒ NEC) requires a continuity hypothesis on `T` because null\n230\tvectors are limits of timelike sequences. We encode the continuity\n231\thypothesis explicitly to keep it visible.\n232\t-/\n233\t\n234\t/--\n235\t**DEC ⇒ WEC** (direct from the DEC bundle's WEC field).\n236\tNon-trivial because the conclusion is the bundle's load-bearing\n237\tsign claim, accessed via projection.\n238\t-/\n239\ttheorem DEC_implies_WEC\n240\t    (T : StressEnergyTensor) (g : MetricTensor) (t : Vec4)\n241\t    (h : DEC T g t) :\n242\t    WEC T g t := h.1\n243\t\n244\t/--\n245\t**WEC ⇒ NEC under continuity hypothesis.** A null vector `k` can be\n246\tapproximated by a sequence of future-directed timelike `v_n → k`\n247\twith `g(v_n, v_n) ↑ 0`. Continuity of `T` on the limit gives\n248\t`T(k, k) ≥ 0`. We encode this as a hypothesis-parameterized theorem:\n249\tgiven an explicit continuity-of-T witness, WEC implies NEC.\n250\t\n251\tThe continuity hypothesis `h_cts` says: for every null `k`, there\n252\texists a sequence of future-directed timelike vectors `v : ℕ → Vec4`\n253\tconverging to `k` with `T(v n, v n) → T(k, k)`.\n254\t\n255\tAnti-pattern audit: the continuity hypothesis is load-bearing —\n256\twithout it, WEC ⇏ NEC because a discontinuous `T` could be\n257\tnon-negative on the open timelike cone but negative on the null\n258\tboundary.\n259\t-/\n260\ttheorem WEC_implies_NEC_under_continuity\n261\t    (T : StressEnergyTensor) (g : MetricTensor) (t : Vec4)\n262\t    (h_wec : WEC T g t)\n263\t    (h_cts : ∀ k : Vec4, IsNull g k →\n264\t      ∃ v : ℕ → Vec4, (∀ n, IsFutureDirectedTimelike g t (v n)) ∧\n265\t        Filter.Tendsto (fun n => T (v n) (v n)) Filter.atTop (nhds (T k k))) :\n266\t    NEC T g := by\n267\t  intro k hk\n268\t  obtain ⟨v, hv_fdtl, hv_lim⟩ := h_cts k hk\n269\t  -- Each T(v n, v n) ≥ 0 by WEC\n270\t  have h_seq_nn : ∀ n, 0 ≤ T (v n) (v n) := fun n => h_wec (v n) (hv_fdtl n)\n271\t  -- Limit of non-negative sequence is non-negative\n272\t  exact ge_of_tendsto' hv_lim (fun n => h_seq_nn n)\n273\t\n274\t/--\n275\t**DEC ⇒ NEC under continuity hypothesis.** Composition of\n276\t`DEC_implies_WEC` and `WEC_implies_NEC_under_continuity`.\n277\t-/\n278\ttheorem DEC_implies_NEC_under_continuity\n279\t    (T : StressEnergyTensor) (g : MetricTensor) (t : Vec4)\n280\t    (h_dec : DEC T g t)\n281\t    (h_cts : ∀ k : Vec4, IsNull g k →\n282\t      ∃ v : ℕ → Vec4, (∀ n, IsFutureDirectedTimelike g t (v n)) ∧\n283\t        Filter.Tendsto (fun n => T (v n) (v n)) Filter.atTop (nhds (T k k))) :\n284\t    NEC T g :=\n285\t  WEC_implies_NEC_under_continuity T g t (DEC_implies_WEC T g t h_dec) h_cts\n286\t\n287\t/-! ## §5 — Counterexample witnesses\n288\t\n289\tThe classical separation theorems require explicit witnesses. We\n290\tconstruct three: cosmological-Λ violates SEC but satisfies WEC/NEC/DEC;\n291\tstiff fluid violates DEC but satisfies WEC/NEC/SEC; ghost scalar\n292\tviolates NEC.\n293\t-/\n294\t\n295\t/--\n296\t**Minkowski metric** (signature `−+++`). Concrete witness for `g`\n297\tin §6 counterexamples.\n298\t-/\n299\tnoncomputable def minkowskiMetric : MetricTensor :=\n300\t  fun u v => -(u 0) * (v 0) + (u 1) * (v 1) + (u 2) * (v 2) + (u 3) * (v 3)\n301\t\n302\t/--\n303\t**Cosmological-Λ stress-energy** with parameter `Λ > 0`. In\n304\tsignature `−+++` with units `8πG = 1`:\n305\t\n306\t    T_μν = -Λ · g_μν,    so   T(u, v) = -Λ · g(u, v).\n307\t\n308\tThis corresponds to `ρ = Λ`, `p = -Λ` (de Sitter equation of state).\n309\tIt satisfies WEC and NEC and DEC, but VIOLATES SEC for `Λ > 0`.\n310\t-/\n311\tnoncomputable def cosmologicalLambdaTensor (Λ : ℝ) : StressEnergyTensor :=\n312\t  fun u v => -Λ * minkowskiMetric u v\n313\t\n314\t/--\n315\t**Cosmological-Λ tensor satisfies WEC** for `Λ ≥ 0`.\n316\t\n317\tProof: `T(v, v) = -Λ · g(v, v)`. For future-directed timelike `v`,\n318\t`g(v, v) < 0`, so `T(v, v) = -Λ · (negative) = Λ · |g(v,v)| ≥ 0`\n319\twhen `Λ ≥ 0`.\n320\t-/\n321\ttheorem cosmologicalLambda_WEC\n322\t    {Λ : ℝ} (hΛ : 0 ≤ Λ) (t : Vec4) :\n323\t    WEC (cosmologicalLambdaTensor Λ) minkowskiMetric t := by\n324\t  intro v ⟨h_tl, _⟩\n325\t  unfold cosmologicalLambdaTensor\n326\t  -- T(v,v) = -Λ · g(v,v); g(v,v) < 0; so -Λ · g(v,v) ≥ 0 when Λ ≥ 0\n327\t  have h_gv_neg : minkowskiMetric v v < 0 := h_tl\n328\t  nlinarith\n329\t\n330\t/--\n331\t**Cosmological-Λ tensor VIOLATES SEC** for `Λ > 0`, where the trace\n332\tis `trT = -4Λ` in 4D (since `T_μν = -Λ g_μν` ⇒ `T^μ_μ = -4Λ`).\n333\t\n334\tProof: SEC requires `T(v,v) - (1/2) · trT · g(v,v) ≥ 0` for\n335\tfuture-directed timelike `v`. Substituting:\n336\t- `T(v,v) = -Λ · g(v,v)`\n337\t- `(1/2) · trT · g(v,v) = (1/2) · (-4Λ) · g(v,v) = -2Λ · g(v,v)`\n338\t- Difference: `-Λ · g(v,v) - (-2Λ · g(v,v)) = Λ · g(v,v)`\n339\t\n340\tFor `Λ > 0` and timelike `v`, `g(v,v) < 0`, so `Λ · g(v,v) < 0`.\n341\tSEC requires `≥ 0`, so SEC is VIOLATED.\n342\t\n343\tWe supply an explicit witness `v_witness = (1, 0, 0, 0)` (future-\n344\tdirected timelike in `−+++` signature with `t = (1,0,0,0)`).\n345\t-/\n346\ttheorem cosmologicalLambda_violates_SEC\n347\t    {Λ : ℝ} (hΛ : 0 < Λ) :\n348\t    let t : Vec4 := ![1, 0, 0, 0]\n349\t    let v_witness : Vec4 := ![1, 0, 0, 0]\n350\t    IsFutureDirectedTimelike minkowskiMetric t v_witness ∧\n351\t    ¬ (0 ≤ (cosmologicalLambdaTensor Λ) v_witness v_witness\n352\t        - (1/2) * (-4 * Λ) * minkowskiMetric v_witness v_witness) := by\n353\t  refine ⟨?_, ?_⟩\n354\t  · refine ⟨?_, ?_⟩\n355\t    · -- IsTimelike: g(v, v) = -1 < 0\n356\t      unfold IsTimelike minkowskiMetric\n357\t      simp [Matrix.cons_val_zero, Matrix.cons_val_one]\n358\t    · -- g(t, v) < 0: with t = v = (1,0,0,0), g(t, v) = -1 < 0 ✓\n359\t      unfold minkowskiMetric\n360\t      simp [Matrix.cons_val_zero, Matrix.cons_val_one]\n361\t  · -- SEC violation: T(v,v) - (1/2)·trT·g(v,v) = Λ·g(v,v) = -Λ < 0\n362\t    unfold cosmologicalLambdaTensor minkowskiMetric\n363\t    simp [Matrix.cons_val_zero, Matrix.cons_val_one]\n364\t    nlinarith\n365\t\n366\t/--\n367\t**Cosmological-Λ tensor satisfies NEC** for `Λ ≥ 0`.\n368\t\n369\tProof: `T(k, k) = -Λ · g(k, k) = 0` for null `k` (since `g(k, k) = 0`\n370\tby the null condition). NEC requires `0 ≤ T(k, k) = 0`, which holds\n371\ttrivially. Note this proof does NOT require `Λ > 0` — even at\n372\t`Λ = 0`, NEC holds (vacuously).\n373\t-/\n374\ttheorem cosmologicalLambda_NEC\n375\t    (Λ : ℝ) :\n376\t    NEC (cosmologicalLambdaTensor Λ) minkowskiMetric := by\n377\t  intro k h_null\n378\t  unfold cosmologicalLambdaTensor\n379\t  -- T(k, k) = -Λ · g(k, k); g(k, k) = 0 from null condition\n380\t  rw [h_null.1]\n381\t  -- Goal: 0 ≤ -Λ * 0 = 0\n382\t  linarith\n383\t\n384\t/-! ## §6 — Ghost-scalar witness: NEC violation\n385\t\n386\tA ghost scalar field has stress-energy `T_μν = -∂_μφ ∂_νφ +\n387\t(1/2) g_μν (∂φ)²`. For null `k`, `T(k, k) = -(k·∂φ)² ≤ 0`, with\n388\tequality only when `k·∂φ = 0`. This is the canonical NEC-violator\n389\tin the literature (Carroll §4.6).\n390\t\n391\tWe construct an explicit ghost-scalar tensor (as a (0,2) bilinear\n392\tform) with non-zero gradient `n` and verify `T(k, k) < 0` for an\n393\texplicit null vector that is not orthogonal to `n`.\n394\t-/\n395\t\n396\t/--\n397\t**Ghost-scalar stress-energy tensor** with field-gradient `n : Vec4`.\n398\tEncoded as `T(u, v) = -⟨n, u⟩·⟨n, v⟩ + (1/2) ⟨n, n⟩_g · g(u, v)`\n399\twhere `⟨n, u⟩ = n 0 · u 0 + n 1 · u 1 + n 2 · u 2 + n 3 · u 3` is\n400\tthe un-metric-contracted inner product (placeholder for `∂_μφ · u^μ`\n401\tin coordinate-free notation).\n402\t\n403\tFor our explicit witness we pick `n` with non-zero components such\n404\tthat the ghost behavior is manifest.\n405\t-/\n406\tnoncomputable def ghostScalarTensor (n : Vec4) : StressEnergyTensor :=\n407\t  fun u v =>\n408\t    -((n 0 * u 0 + n 1 * u 1 + n 2 * u 2 + n 3 * u 3)\n409\t       * (n 0 * v 0 + n 1 * v 1 + n 2 * v 2 + n 3 * v 3))\n410\t\n411\t/--\n412\t**Ghost-scalar VIOLATES NEC** with an explicit witness.\n413\t\n414\tWe pick `n = (0, 1, 0, 0)` (purely spacelike gradient) and\n415\t`k = (1, 1, 0, 0)` (null vector: `g(k, k) = -1 + 1 = 0`).\n416\tThen `(n · k) = 0·1 + 1·1 = 1`, so `T(k, k) = -(1)² = -1 < 0`.\n417\tNEC requires `0 ≤ T(k, k)`, so it is VIOLATED.\n418\t\n419\tNote: we use the simplified `T(u, v) = -⟨n, u⟩·⟨n, v⟩` form (drop\n420\tthe `(1/2) g(...)` term, which would also vanish on null `k`); the\n421\tNEC-violation content is preserved under either form.\n422\t-/\n423\ttheorem ghostScalar_violates_NEC :\n424\t    let n : Vec4 := ![0, 1, 0, 0]\n425\t    let k_null : Vec4 := ![1, 1, 0, 0]\n426\t    IsNull minkowskiMetric k_null ∧\n427\t    (ghostScalarTensor n) k_null k_null < 0 := by\n428\t  refine ⟨?_, ?_⟩\n429\t  · -- IsNull: g(k, k) = -1 + 1 = 0, AND k ≠ 0\n430\t    refine ⟨?_, ?_⟩\n431\t    · unfold minkowskiMetric\n432\t      simp [Matrix.cons_val_zero, Matrix.cons_val_one]\n433\t    · intro h\n434\t      have h0 : (![1, 1, 0, 0] : Vec4) 0 = 0 := by rw [h]; rfl\n435\t      simp [Matrix.cons_val_zero] at h0\n436\t  · -- T(k, k) = -1 < 0\n437\t    unfold ghostScalarTensor\n438\t    simp [Matrix.cons_val_zero, Matrix.cons_val_one]\n439\t\n440\t/-! ## §6.5 — Perfect-fluid carrier and stiff-fluid DEC-violation witness\n441\t\n442\tA perfect-fluid stress-energy tensor in the rest frame `u = (1,0,0,0)`\n443\ttakes the diagonal form `T_μν = diag(ρ, p, p, p)` (where `ρ` is energy\n444\tdensity and `p` is pressure). Per Hawking-Ellis Type I §4.3, this is\n445\tthe canonical setting for predicate-equivalence theorems and\n446\tcounterexamples.\n447\t\n448\tWe supply an explicit perfect-fluid tensor in the rest frame and use\n449\tit to provide the **stiff-fluid DEC-violation witness** named in\n450\tthe Phase 6f deep-research audit §5.6f.3: `ρ = 1`, `p = 2` satisfies\n451\tWEC + NEC + SEC but VIOLATES DEC.\n452\t-/\n453\t\n454\t/--\n455\t**Perfect-fluid stress-energy tensor in rest frame `u = (1,0,0,0)`,\n456\tsignature `−+++`.** Components `T_μν = diag(ρ, p, p, p)` (in this\n457\tframe); evaluated as a bilinear form on `Vec4`,\n458\t`T(v, w) = ρ · v⁰ · w⁰ + p · (v¹·w¹ + v²·w² + v³·w³)`.\n459\t\n460\tFor arbitrary 4-velocity `u` the formula is `T_μν = (ρ + p) u_μ u_ν +\n461\tp g_μν`; we specialize to the rest-frame here for explicitness.\n462\t-/\n463\tnoncomputable def perfectFluidTensor (ρ p : ℝ) : StressEnergyTensor :=\n464\t  fun v w =>\n465\t    ρ * (v 0) * (w 0) + p * ((v 1) * (w 1) + (v 2) * (w 2) + (v 3) * (w 3))\n466\t\n467\t/--\n468\t**Stiff-fluid VIOLATES DEC** with the audit's named witness `ρ = 1,\n469\tp = 2`.\n470\t\n471\tConcrete witness pair: `v = (1, 9/10, 0, 0)`, `w = (1, -9/10, 0, 0)`\n472\t(both future-directed timelike in `−+++` with `t = (1,0,0,0)`:\n473\t`g(v, v) = -1 + 81/100 = -19/100 < 0`, `g(t, v) = -1 < 0`; same for `w`).\n474\t\n475\t`T(v, w) = ρ·1·1 + p · ((9/10)·(-9/10) + 0 + 0)\n476\t        = 1 + 2 · (-81/100)\n477\t        = 1 - 162/100 = -62/100 = -31/50 < 0`,\n478\t\n479\tso DEC's `T(v, w) ≥ 0` requirement is VIOLATED.\n480\t\n481\tAnti-pattern audit: explicit numerical witnesses (no ∃-absorption);\n482\tboth `v` and `w` are independently future-directed timelike (so the\n483\tviolation isn't due to a degenerate one of them); the ratio `p > ρ`\n484\tis exactly the audit's flagged stiff-fluid regime (`ρ < |p|`).\n485\t-/\n486\ttheorem stiff_fluid_violates_DEC :\n487\t    let t : Vec4 := ![1, 0, 0, 0]\n488\t    let v_witness : Vec4 := ![1, 9/10, 0, 0]\n489\t    let w_witness : Vec4 := ![1, -9/10, 0, 0]\n490\t    IsFutureDirectedTimelike minkowskiMetric t v_witness ∧\n491\t    IsFutureDirectedTimelike minkowskiMetric t w_witness ∧\n492\t    (perfectFluidTensor 1 2) v_witness w_witness < 0 := by\n493\t  refine ⟨?_, ?_, ?_⟩\n494\t  · -- v is future-directed timelike\n495\t    refine ⟨?_, ?_⟩\n496\t    · unfold IsTimelike minkowskiMetric\n497\t      simp [Matrix.cons_val_zero, Matrix.cons_val_one]\n498\t      norm_num\n499\t    · unfold minkowskiMetric\n500\t      simp [Matrix.cons_val_zero, Matrix.cons_val_one]\n501\t  · -- w is future-directed timelike\n502\t    refine ⟨?_, ?_⟩\n503\t    · unfold IsTimelike minkowskiMetric\n504\t      simp [Matrix.cons_val_zero, Matrix.cons_val_one]\n505\t      norm_num\n506\t    · unfold minkowskiMetric\n507\t      simp [Matrix.cons_val_zero, Matrix.cons_val_one]\n508\t  · -- T(v, w) = -31/50 < 0\n509\t    unfold perfectFluidTensor\n510\t    simp [Matrix.cons_val_zero, Matrix.cons_val_one]\n511\t    norm_num\n512\t\n513\t/-! ## §7 — Module summary -/\n514\t\n515\t/--\n516\t**Phase 6f Wave 3 module summary marker.** This module ships **first\n517\tformalization** (per the Phase 6f deep-research audit §3E) of the\n518\tfour classical-GR energy conditions WEC/NEC/DEC/SEC as predicates on\n519\tan abstract bilinear form, with chain implications and explicit\n520\tcounterexample witnesses (cosmological-Λ violates SEC; stiff-fluid\n521\tviolates DEC; ghost-scalar violates NEC).\n522\t\n523\tTheorem roster: 8 substantive theorems + 1 marker, 0 sorry,\n524\t0 new axioms.\n525\t\n526\t- `DEC_implies_WEC` (chain implication, direct projection)\n527\t- `WEC_implies_NEC_under_continuity` (chain via continuity)\n528\t- `DEC_implies_NEC_under_continuity` (composition)\n529\t- `cosmologicalLambda_WEC` (Λ ≥ 0 satisfies WEC)\n530\t- `cosmologicalLambda_violates_SEC` (Λ > 0 violates SEC, explicit witness)\n531\t- `cosmologicalLambda_NEC` (Λ at any value satisfies NEC vacuously)\n532\t- `ghostScalar_violates_NEC` (NEC violation via explicit non-zero\n533\t  gradient and explicit null vector)\n534\t- `stiff_fluid_violates_DEC` (DEC violation for ρ=1, p=2 with\n535\t  explicit witness pair v=(1,9/10,0,0), w=(1,-9/10,0,0))\n536\t\n537\t**Anti-pattern audit (4-pattern check):** all counterexample\n538\twitnesses are explicit (no ∃-absorption); WEC predicate has\n539\tload-bearing timelike restriction (no P4 vacuous); chain\n540\timplications carry continuity hypothesis explicitly; counterexample\n541\tproofs use explicit numerical witnesses + `norm_num` / `nlinarith`.\n542\t\n543\t**Cross-layer Python pipeline (Phase 6f W3 backfill 2026-04-29):**\n544\t`tests/test_energy_conditions.py` mirrors the 8 substantive theorems\n545\t(38 pytest cases / 7 test classes). `formulas.py` ships predicate\n546\thelpers (`is_null_vec`, `is_timelike`, `is_future_directed_timelike`)\n547\tand condition checks (`nec_check`, `wec_check`, `dec_check`,\n548\t`sec_check`) plus the three named tensor witnesses\n549\t(`cosmological_lambda_stress_energy`, `ghost_scalar_stress_energy`,\n550\t`perfect_fluid_stress_energy`). Figure\n551\t`fig_energy_conditions_perfect_fluid_regions` visualizes the four\n552\tcondition regions in the (ρ, p) plane with the cos-Λ + stiff-fluid\n553\twitnesses marked.\n554\t-/\n555\ttheorem _phase6f_w3_module_summary_marker : True := trivial\n556\t\n557\tend SKEFTHawking.EnergyConditions\n558\t"}]},"uuid":"6f463240-af45-4483-ae6f-6538695dcfcd","timestamp":"2026-08-15T19:40:41.014Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"e1ea857c-0a3a-4375-8582-49c75f40b415","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/johnroehm/Programming/PythonEnvironments/Physics/Fluid-Based-Physics-Research/SK_EFT_Hawking","sessionId":"6cf37aa2-f1c7-4e33-a712-f17c2d75d99b","version":"2.1.229","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"swift-singing-llama"}

{"parentUuid":"071a3e7b-7b4c-453a-94db-d79ab713c8d4","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"d7b89794-41f0-46fe-9a20-6d198f8f624f","agentId":"a1b16a56a1a83bc81","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01KRZs3aCf9y2Uxt1dh8VNF3","type":"tool_result","content":"1\timport SKEFTHawking.Basic\n2\timport SKEFTHawking.BundleRiemannAux\n3\timport Mathlib\n4\t\n5\t/-!\n6\t# Phase 6f Wave 8 Session 4a — Cyclic Jacobi for `mlieBracket` + abstract bundle first Bianchi\n7\t\n8\t## Overview\n9\t\n10\tSession 4a of the 5-session Wave 8 plan. We extend Session 3's\n11\t`bundleRiemannAux` (`R(X, Y) Z := ∇_X ∇_Y Z − ∇_Y ∇_X Z − ∇_{[X, Y]} Z`)\n12\twith the **algebraic first Bianchi identity** under torsion-freeness, when\n13\tthe bundle is the tangent bundle (so the cyclic permutation\n14\t`R(X,Y)Z + R(Y,Z)X + R(Z,X)Y` is well-typed).\n15\t\n16\tThe mathematical core of the first Bianchi proof is the **cyclic Jacobi\n17\tidentity** for the manifold Lie bracket:\n18\t\n19\t  `[X, [Y, Z]] + [Y, [Z, X]] + [Z, [X, Y]] = 0`.\n20\t\n21\tMathlib's `leibniz_identity_mlieBracket` provides the equivalent\n22\t`[U, [V, W]] = [[U, V], W] + [V, [U, W]]` (Leibniz form). The cyclic form\n23\tfollows from one Leibniz instance plus three antisymmetry rewrites — outer\n24\tswap (`mlieBracket_swap`) and inner-negation distribution. Mathlib4 has\n25\tneither cyclic Jacobi nor `mlieBracket_neg_right_apply` at `8850ed93`,\n26\tso this module ships pointwise versions of both.\n27\t\n28\t## Wave-headline content\n29\t\n30\t1. **`mlieBracket_neg_right_apply` / `mlieBracket_neg_left_apply`** —\n31\t   pointwise `[V, -W]_x = -[V, W]_x` and `[-V, W]_x = -[V, W]_x`, derived\n32\t   from `mlieBracket_const_smul_right` / `_left` with `c := (-1 : 𝕜)` and\n33\t   `neg_one_smul`.\n34\t\n35\t2. **`mlieBracket_cyclic_jacobi_apply`** — pointwise cyclic Jacobi, derived\n36\t   from one `leibniz_identity_mlieBracket_apply` instance + outer-swap +\n37\t   inner-neg-distribute.\n38\t\n39\t3. **`bundleRiemannAux_first_bianchi_torsionfree_apply`** — for `cov` a\n40\t   covariant derivative on the tangent bundle (V := TangentSpace I) that\n41\t   is torsion-free in the sense of Bonn's\n42\t   `cov W x (V x) − cov V x (W x) = mlieBracket I V W x` characterization,\n43\t   the cyclic sum `R(X,Y)Z + R(Y,Z)X + R(Z,X)Y = 0` falls out from\n44\t   cyclic Jacobi via three torsion-free rewrites.\n45\t\n46\t## Why bundle scope (architectural payoff)\n47\t\n48\tSession 1 proved coordinate-level Riemann via direct expansion of the\n49\tChristoffel-symbol formula plus ~500 `ring`-monomials of cancellation.\n50\tSession 4a's bundle-level first Bianchi is **3-line Jacobi** plus a\n51\ttorsion-free regrouping. This is the main payoff of moving to bundle\n52\tscope: the Bianchi identities reduce to algebraic properties of the\n53\t**Lie bracket** on vector fields, with the Christoffel-style cancellation\n54\tabsorbed into the type-level continuous linearity of `cov σ x` and the\n55\tBonn-API shape of torsion-freeness.\n56\t\n57\tSession 4b lifts `bundleRiemannAux` to a fiber-trilinear\n58\t`CovariantDerivative.riemann` via `TensorialAt.mkHom₃` (which we will\n59\tbuild, since pinned Mathlib only ships `mkHom` and `mkHom₂`); Session 5\n60\tdelivers Levi-Civita uniqueness at bundle level via the Koszul-bilinear\n61\tform argument.\n62\t\n63\t## References\n64\t\n65\t- P. Massot, M. Rothgang, H. Macbeth, *CovariantDerivative + Torsion*\n66\t  (Mathlib4 2025; landed in pinned `8850ed93`).\n67\t- S. Gouëzel et al., *Lie brackets of vector fields on manifolds*\n68\t  (Mathlib4):\n69\t  - `Mathlib.Geometry.Manifold.VectorField.LieBracket`:\n70\t    `mlieBracket_swap`, `mlieBracket_swap_apply`,\n71\t    `mlieBracket_const_smul_right`, `mlieBracket_const_smul_left`,\n72\t    `leibniz_identity_mlieBracket`, `leibniz_identity_mlieBracket_apply`.\n73\t- S. Kobayashi & K. Nomizu, *Foundations of Differential Geometry* Vol. I\n74\t  (1963), Theorem III.5.1 (algebraic first Bianchi for torsion-free\n75\t  connections on the tangent bundle).\n76\t- W. Klingenberg, *Riemannian Geometry* (2nd ed., 1995), §1.3.\n77\t\n78\t**First formalization in any proof assistant** (per Phase 6f audit §3E)\n79\tof cyclic Jacobi for the manifold Lie bracket and the bundle-level\n80\tfirst Bianchi for torsion-free covariant derivatives on the tangent\n81\tbundle. Mathlib4 ships only the Leibniz form, not cyclic Jacobi; no\n82\tother system (Coq, Isabelle/AFP, HOL Light, HOL4, Mizar, Agda) has\n83\teither.\n84\t-/\n85\t\n86\t@[expose] public section\n87\t\n88\tnamespace SKEFTHawking.BundleRiemann\n89\t\n90\topen Bundle NormedSpace\n91\topen VectorField\n92\topen scoped Manifold ContDiff Topology\n93\t\n94\t/-! ## Variable section\n95\t\n96\tWe follow Bonn's `CovariantDerivative.Basic` setup. Note the\n97\t`[IsManifold I (minSmoothness 𝕜 3) M] [CompleteSpace E]` instances —\n98\tMathlib's Leibniz section requires these.\n99\t-/\n100\t\n101\tvariable {𝕜 : Type*} [NontriviallyNormedField 𝕜]\n102\t  {E : Type*} [NormedAddCommGroup E] [NormedSpace 𝕜 E]\n103\t  {H : Type*} [TopologicalSpace H] {I : ModelWithCorners 𝕜 E H}\n104\t  {M : Type*} [TopologicalSpace M] [ChartedSpace H M]\n105\t  [IsManifold I (minSmoothness 𝕜 3) M] [CompleteSpace E]\n106\t\n107\t/-! ## §1 Negation lemmas for `mlieBracket`\n108\t\n109\tMathlib4 at `8850ed93` ships `mlieBracket_const_smul_right` /\n110\t`mlieBracket_const_smul_left` (scalar multiplication in either argument\n111\tunder a smoothness hypothesis), `mlieBracket_swap` / `_swap_apply`\n112\t(antisymmetry), but no direct pointwise negation-distribute lemma.\n113\t\n114\tWe derive the negation lemmas via `c := (-1 : 𝕜) • _ = -_` plus\n115\t`mlieBracket_const_smul_*`. The pointwise version requires `MDiffAt\n116\t(T% W) x` (resp. V); the section-level version needs section-level\n117\tdifferentiability.\n118\t-/\n119\t\n120\t/-- Pointwise negation distribution in the second argument:\n121\t`[V, -W]_x = -[V, W]_x`. Derived from `mlieBracket_const_smul_right`\n122\twith `c := (-1 : 𝕜)` and `neg_one_smul`. -/\n123\tlemma mlieBracket_neg_right_apply\n124\t    {V W : Π x : M, TangentSpace I x} {x : M}\n125\t    (hW : MDiffAt (T% W) x) :\n126\t    mlieBracket I V (-W) x = -mlieBracket I V W x := by\n127\t  have h := mlieBracket_const_smul_right (V := V) (c := (-1 : 𝕜)) hW\n128\t  -- h : mlieBracket I V ((-1 : 𝕜) • W) x = (-1 : 𝕜) • mlieBracket I V W x\n129\t  -- (-1 : 𝕜) • W = -W and (-1 : 𝕜) • mlieBracket I V W x = -mlieBracket I V W x\n130\t  have eW : ((-1 : 𝕜) • W : Π x : M, TangentSpace I x) = -W := by\n131\t    funext y; exact neg_one_smul 𝕜 (W y)\n132\t  rw [eW] at h\n133\t  rw [h, neg_one_smul]\n134\t\n135\t/-- Pointwise negation distribution in the first argument:\n136\t`[-V, W]_x = -[V, W]_x`. Derived from `mlieBracket_const_smul_left`\n137\twith `c := (-1 : 𝕜)` and `neg_one_smul`. -/\n138\tlemma mlieBracket_neg_left_apply\n139\t    {V W : Π x : M, TangentSpace I x} {x : M}\n140\t    (hV : MDiffAt (T% V) x) :"}]},"uuid":"9900a5b3-41fc-4d51-9c3f-dedf386b6803","timestamp":"2026-08-15T19:40:46.296Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"071a3e7b-7b4c-453a-94db-d79ab713c8d4","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/johnroehm/Programming/PythonEnvironments/Physics/Fluid-Based-Physics-Research/SK_EFT_Hawking","sessionId":"6cf37aa2-f1c7-4e33-a712-f17c2d75d99b","version":"2.1.229","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"swift-singing-llama"}

exact htf X Y y\n441-  -- Per-axis \"group\" equality: pairing the two double-derivative terms\n442-  -- with the matching connection-on-bracket term collapses to a\n443-  -- bracket-of-brackets via `hcov.sub` + two htf applications.\n444-  have hX_grp :\n445-      cov (fun y => cov Z y (Y y)) x (X x) - cov (fun y => cov Y y (Z y)) x (X x)\n446-        - cov X x (mlieBracket I Y Z x) = mlieBracket I X (mlieBracket I Y Z) x := by\n447-    have h1 : cov (fun y => cov Z y (Y y)) x (X x) - cov (fun y => cov Y y (Z y)) x (X x)\n448-            = cov (mlieBracket I Y Z) x (X x) := by\n449-      rw [← ContinuousLinearMap.sub_apply, ← (hcov.sub h_YZ_sec h_ZY_sec), hYZ_sec_eq]\n450-    rw [h1]; exact htf X (mlieBracket I Y Z) x\n451-  have hY_grp :\n452-      cov (fun y => cov X y (Z y)) x (Y x) - cov (fun y => cov Z y (X y)) x (Y x)\n453-        - cov Y x (mlieBracket I Z X x) = mlieBracket I Y (mlieBracket I Z X) x := by\n454-    have h1 : cov (fun y => cov X y (Z y)) x (Y x) - cov (fun y => cov Z y (X y)) x (Y x)\n455-            = cov (mlieBracket I Z X) x (Y x) := by\n456-      rw [← ContinuousLinearMap.sub_apply, ← (hcov.sub h_ZX_sec h_XZ_sec), hZX_sec_eq]\n457-    rw [h1]; All rights reserved.\n3\tReleased under Apache 2.0 license as described in the LICENSE file.\n4\t\n5\tPhase 6g.4 substantive curve-theoretic Wave 9 Session 4 — Hawking area\n6\ttheorem at the curve-theoretic null-generator-evolution scope.\n7\t\n8\tDischarges the 6g.4 substantive curve-theoretic gap: the Hawking 1971\n9\tarea theorem (Hawking 1971 *Phys. Rev. Lett.* 26 1344) at the curve-\n10\tlevel null-generator scope. The substantive content is the\n11\t**area-evolution monotone-rigidity theorem**: if a horizon area\n12\t`A(λ)` along a null generator satisfies the area-evolution equation\n13\t`A'(λ) = θ(λ) · A(λ)` with non-negative expansion `θ(λ) ≥ 0` (the\n14\tPenrose-1968 classical-area-monotonicity theorem under NEC + cosmic\n15\tcensorship hypothesis), then `A(λ)` is non-decreasing in the affine\n16\tparameter `λ`.\n17\t\n18\t**Mathematical content (Hawking 1971 / Wald §12.2 / Hawking-Ellis 1973\n19\t§9.2):** the null-generator congruence on the future event horizon has\n20\texpansion `θ` satisfying the Raychaudhuri equation:\n21\t\n22\t    dθ/dλ  =  −θ²/(n−2) − σ² − R_kk,\n23\t\n24\twhere `R_kk = R_μν k^μ k^ν ≥ 0` (NEC) and `σ²`, `θ²/(n−2)` are\n25\tnon-negative. Cosmic censorship (no naked singularities) forbids the\n26\tgenerators from terminating at finite `λ`, so the focusing inequality\n27\t`θ' ≤ −θ²/(n−2) − R_kk ≤ 0` cannot drive `θ → −∞` at finite `λ` (the\n28\tgenerator would be removed from the horizon if it focused). Hence\n29\t`θ ≥ 0` on the horizon (Hawking 1971 Lemma).\n30\t\n31\tGiven `θ ≥ 0`, the area density along a null generator evolves as\n32\t`A'(λ) = θ(λ) · A(λ)`, which is non-negative for `A ≥ 0`. Hence the\n33\tarea is monotone-non-decreasing.\n34\t\n35\tThe 1D real-analysis distillation: under the area-evolution ODE\n36\t`A'(λ) = θ(λ) · A(λ)` with `A(λ) ≥ 0` and `θ(λ) ≥ 0`, conclude\n37\t`MonotoneOn A [0, L]`.\n38\t\n39\t**Bundle-target alignment:** lifts as **D3 §26** (the area-theorem\n40\tsection of the correctness-push bundle) per `PAPER_DRAFT_MAPPING.md`\n41\tPhase 6g addendum. Cross-bridge to the existing 6g.4 algebraic-\n42\tprecedent `AreaTheorem.schwarzschild_area_monotone` infrastructure.\n43\t\n44\t**First formalization in any proof assistant** (per Phase 6f audit\n45\t§3E + this session's audit) of the Hawking area theorem at the\n46\tcurve-theoretic null-generator-evolution scope. Mathlib has\n47\t`monotoneOn_of_deriv_nonneg` but no combined area-evolution-ODE +\n48\tNEC-controlled-expansion content; no other proof assistant has the\n49\tchain in any form per the Phase 6f audit §3E.\n50\t-/\n51\timport SKEFTHawking.AreaTheorem\n52\timport Mathlib.Analysis.Calculus.Deriv.MeanValue\n53\t\n54\tnamespace SKEFTHawking.AreaTheoremCurveTheoretic\n55\t\n56\topen Set\n57\topen SKEFTHawking.AreaTheorem\n58\t\n59\t/-! ## §1 Curve-level area-evolution predicate\n60\t\n61\tWe encode the curve-level area-evolution hypothesis on the parametric\n62\tinterval `[0, L]`: a real-valued area function `A : ℝ → ℝ` and an\n63\texpansion function `θ : ℝ → ℝ` with the area-evolution ODE `A'(λ) =\n64\tθ(λ) · A(λ)`. The substantive content is the encoded NEC + cosmic-\n65\tcensorship reduction to `θ ≥ 0` (the Hawking-1971 lemma), which\n66\tcombined with `A ≥ 0` gives `A' ≥ 0`.\n67\t-/\n68\t\n69\t/--\n70\t**`IsHorizonAreaCurve A θ L`:** the curve-level area-evolution\n71\thypothesis on the parametric interval `[0, L]`. The functions\n72\t`A : ℝ → ℝ` (horizon area along a null generator) and `θ : ℝ → ℝ`\n73\t(expansion of the null generator) satisfy:\n74\t- `domain_nonneg` — the parametric interval `[0, L]` is non-degenerate\n75\t  (`0 ≤ L`);\n76\t- `A_nonneg` — `0 ≤ A(λ)` on `[0, L]` (physical area);\n77\t- `A_continuous` — `A` is continuous on `[0, L]`;\n78\t- `A_evolution` — the area-evolution ODE `A'(λ) = θ(λ) · A(λ)` on\n79\t  `(0, L)` (the area's logarithmic derivative is the expansion);\n80\t- `theta_nonneg` — `θ(λ) ≥ 0` on `(0, L)`, encoding the NEC + cosmic-\n81\t  censorship reduction (the Hawking-1971 lemma).\n82\t-/\n83\tstructure IsHorizonAreaCurve (A θ : ℝ → ℝ) (L : ℝ) : Prop where\n84\t  /-- The parametric interval `[0, L]` is non-degenerate. -/\n85\t  domain_nonneg : (0 : ℝ) ≤ L\n86\t  /-- The horizon area is physical (non-negative). -/\n87\t  A_nonneg : ∀ lam ∈ Set.Icc (0 : ℝ) L, 0 ≤ A lam\n88\t  /-- Continuity on the closed parametric interval. -/\n89\t  A_continuous : ContinuousOn A (Set.Icc (0 : ℝ) L)\n90\t  /-- Area-evolution ODE: `A'(λ) = θ(λ) · A(λ)` on `(0, L)`. -/\n91\t  A_evolution : ∀ lam ∈ Set.Ioo (0 : ℝ) L,\n92\t      HasDerivAt A (θ lam * A lam) lam\n93\t  /-- NEC + cosmic-censorship: `θ(λ) ≥ 0` on `(0, L)`. -/\n94\t  theta_nonneg : ∀ lam ∈ Set.Ioo (0 : ℝ) L, 0 ≤ θ lam\n95\t\n96\t/-! ## §2 Wave-headline curve-theoretic area-monotonicity theorem\n97\t\n98\tThe substantive area-monotonicity: under `IsHorizonAreaCurve A θ L`,\n99\tthe horizon area `A` is monotone-non-decreasing on `[0, L]`.\n100\t-/\n101\t\n102\t/--\n103\t**Curve-theoretic area-monotonicity theorem (Wave 9 Session 4\n104\theadline).** Under the curve-level area-evolution hypothesis\n105\t`IsHorizonAreaCurve A θ L`, the horizon area `A` is monotone-non-\n106\tdecreasing on `[0, L]`.\n107\t\n108\t**Substantive content:** this is the curve-theoretic 1D distillation\n109\tof the Hawking 1971 area theorem. The non-negativity of the expansion\n110\t(NEC + cosmic-censorship reduction) plus the area-evolution ODE\n111\t`A' = θ · A` produces `A' = θ · A ≥ 0` (product of two non-negatives),\n112\twhich together with continuity implies monotone-non-decrease.\n113\t\n114\t**Proof:** the area-evolution ODE gives `A'(λ) = θ(λ) · A(λ)` on the\n115\tinterior. Both `θ(λ) ≥ 0` and `A(λ) ≥ 0` (interior ⊂ closed interval),\n116\tso `A'(λ) ≥ 0` on the interior. Apply `monotoneOn_of_deriv_nonneg`.\n117\t-/\n118\ttheorem horizon_area_curve_monotone\n119\t    {A θ : ℝ → ℝ} {L : ℝ}\n120\t    (h : IsHorizonAreaCurve A θ L) :\n121\t    MonotoneOn A (Set.Icc (0 : ℝ) L) := by\n122\t  -- Step 1: interior (Icc 0 L) = Ioo 0 L\n123\t  have h_int_eq : interior (Icc (0 : ℝ) L) = Ioo 0 L := by\n124\t    by_cases hL_pos : 0 < L\n125\t    · exact interior_Icc\n126\t    · rw [not_lt] at hL_pos\n127\t      have hL_eq : L = 0 := le_antisymm hL_pos h.domain_nonneg\n128\t      simp [hL_eq]\n129\t  -- Step 2: DifferentiableOn on the interior\n130\t  have h_diff_on : DifferentiableOn ℝ A (interior (Icc (0 : ℝ) L)) := by"}]},"uuid":"c112884c-4ba9-46ec-bf7a-ea9b4e3e1023","timestamp":"2026-08-15T19:41:11.305Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"f9e1f57d-a143-4c4e-adb5-a57d05fb5b81","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/johnroehm/Programming/PythonEnvironments/Physics/Fluid-Based-Physics-Research/SK_EFT_Hawking","sessionId":"6cf37aa2-f1c7-4e33-a712-f17c2d75d99b","version":"2.1.229","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"swift-singing-llama"}

{"parentUuid":"102d7778-7d44-43c5-b802-8497c2a66780","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"d7b89794-41f0-46fe-9a20-6d198f8f624f","agentId":"a1b16a56a1a83bc81","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01HKZvF2LYN61Hag5zHLkRWD","type":"tool_result","content":"1\timport SKEFTHawking.Basic\n2\timport SKEFTHawking.Curvature\n3\timport SKEFTHawking.LinearizedEFE\n4\timport Mathlib\n5\t\n6\t/-!\n7\t# Phase 6f Wave 7 — Lorentzian Metric (algebraic-precedent + bundle skeleton)\n8\t\n9\t## Overview\n10\t\n11\tPhase 6f catch-up wave (6f.7) — the upstream-Mathlib-style Lorentzian\n12\tmetric infrastructure that Mathlib lacks. Mathlib (commit `8850ed93`,\n13\t2026-04-29 audit) ships positive-definite `IsContinuousRiemannianBundle`\n14\t+ `IsContMDiffRiemannianBundle` (Gouëzel 2025) but NO Lorentzian\n15\tanalog: the indefinite-signature work is explicitly out of HALF ERC\n16\tscope and is assigned to SK-EFT as upstream contributor.\n17\t\n18\tThis module ships the Lorentzian companion at the **algebraic-precedent\n19\tscope** (matching the project's 6f.1-6f.6 + 6g.1-6g.6 pattern):\n20\tpredicate-level signature classification on a `MetricMatrix`\n21\t(`Fin 4 → Fin 4 → ℝ`, the existing 6f.1 carrier) plus a Mathlib-style\n22\ttypeclass `IsContinuousLorentzianBundle` defined via existence of a\n23\tcontinuous Lorentzian-signature bilinear form per fiber.\n24\t\n25\tThe Minkowski metric `LinearizedEFE.η` is the canonical concrete\n26\twitness; the bridge theorem `η_isLorentzianSignature` confirms it has\n27\tsignature (-, +, +, +) and the bundle-level instance lifts this to a\n28\ttrivial vector bundle.\n29\t\n30\t## Scoping mode\n31\t\n32\tPredicate + typeclass + algebraic-precedent witness. The substantive\n33\tcontent is in:\n34\t- `IsLorentzianSignature` — diagonal-form signature predicate\n35\t- `IsLorentzianMetric` — symmetry + non-degeneracy + signature bundle\n36\t- `η_isLorentzianMetric` — Minkowski as canonical witness\n37\t- `IsContinuousLorentzianBundle` — Mathlib-style typeclass mirroring\n38\t  `IsContinuousRiemannianBundle`\n39\t- Bridge theorem: a Riemannian bundle is NOT a Lorentzian bundle\n40\t  (signatures incompatible — first-formalization-level falsifier).\n41\t\n42\t## Anti-pattern audit (per project preemptive-strengthening discipline)\n43\t\n44\t1. **No P1 ∃-absorption:** `IsLorentzianSignature` predicate uses\n45\t   explicit diagonal form, not opaque `∃` over basis.\n46\t2. **No P2 bundle redundancy:** `IsLorentzianMetric` bundles symmetry\n47\t   + non-degeneracy + signature, but each conjunct is independently\n48\t   substantive (a Riemannian metric satisfies symmetry + non-degeneracy\n49\t   but fails signature; a degenerate symmetric form satisfies symmetry\n50\t   + signature-on-non-null-vectors but fails non-degeneracy).\n51\t3. **No P3 trivial-mult-as-physics:** the `η_isLorentzianMetric`\n52\t   witness theorem requires checking signature on the diagonal — a\n53\t   non-trivial `fin_cases` discharge.\n54\t4. **No P4 vacuous axioms:** `IsContinuousLorentzianBundle` is the\n55\t   typeclass-shape Mathlib-PR target; non-vacuous via the trivial\n56\t   Minkowski-bundle instance.\n57\t5. **No P5 falsifier-restating-hypothesis:** the falsifier\n58\t   `riemannian_not_lorentzian_signature` consumes\n59\t   `IsLorentzianSignature` and produces a contradiction with positive\n60\t   definiteness — substantive, not tautological.\n61\t6. **Cross-module bridge integrity P6:** body imports\n62\t   `SKEFTHawking.Curvature` (calls `MetricMatrix`, `MetricSymmetric`)\n63\t   and `SKEFTHawking.LinearizedEFE` (calls `η`, `η_zero_zero`,\n64\t   `η_spatial_diag`, `η_off_diag`, `η_symm` directly).\n65\t\n66\t## References\n67\t\n68\t- C. Misner, K. Thorne, J. Wheeler, *Gravitation* (1973), §13.5.\n69\t- R.M. Wald, *General Relativity* (1984) Appendix.\n70\t- S. Gouëzel 2025, `Mathlib.Topology.VectorBundle.Riemannian` (template\n71\t  for the typeclass shape).\n72\t- Phase 6f deep-research audit §3E + §5.6f.7 (this module's\n73\t  load-bearing scoping).\n74\t\n75\t## Cross-module landscape\n76\t\n77\tThis module is the algebraic foundation for Phase 6f.7 wave content:\n78\t- `RiemannianConnection.lean` (6f.7 second module) — consumes\n79\t  `IsLorentzianMetric` for the metric-compatibility predicate on\n80\t  Lorentzian backgrounds.\n81\t- Phase 6g modules (`CausalStructure.lean`, `PenroseSingularity.lean`,\n82\t  etc.) — would consume `IsLorentzianMetric` to upgrade their abstract\n83\t  `Spacetime`-axiom scope to a concrete metric-witness-backed scope.\n84\t  This bridge work is parked behind further Phase 6 development.\n85\t- Phase 6f.4 `ExactSolutions.lean` — Schwarzschild/dS/Kerr metric\n86\t  matrices can claim `IsLorentzianMetric` via the diagonal-signature\n87\t  bridge (deferred to a dedicated specialization).\n88\t\n89\t**First formalization in any proof assistant** of the Lorentzian\n90\tmetric typeclass with Minkowski witness + Riemannian-Lorentzian\n91\tsignature falsifier (per audit §3E: zero proof assistants currently\n92\thave indefinite-signature metric infrastructure).\n93\t-/\n94\t\n95\t@[expose] public section\n96\t\n97\tnamespace SKEFTHawking.LorentzianMetric\n98\t\n99\topen SKEFTHawking.Curvature\n100\topen SKEFTHawking.LinearizedEFE\n101\t\n102\t/-! ## §1 — Algebraic-precedent: signature predicate on `MetricMatrix` -/\n103\t\n104\t/--\n105\t**`IsLorentzianSignature`:** a metric matrix `g : Fin 4 → Fin 4 → ℝ` has\n106\tLorentzian signature `(−, +, +, +)` if its diagonal entries are\n107\t`(−1, +1, +1, +1)` and its off-diagonal entries vanish — i.e., it\n108\tequals the canonical Minkowski metric in matrix form.\n109\t\n110\tThis is the strict diagonal form. A general Lorentzian metric (not\n111\tdiagonal at the chart) is captured by `IsLorentzianMetric` (§3) below\n112\twhich uses non-degeneracy + symmetry + an existence-of-orthonormal-frame\n113\tpredicate. The strict-diagonal form here is the natural shape for\n114\tlocal-frame / orthonormal-tetrad analyses.\n115\t-/\n116\tdef IsLorentzianSignature (g : MetricMatrix) : Prop :=\n117\t  g 0 0 = -1 ∧\n118\t  (∀ μ : Fin 4, μ ≠ 0 → g μ μ = 1) ∧\n119\t  (∀ μ ν : Fin 4, μ ≠ ν → g μ ν = 0)\n120\t"}]},"uuid":"747af1a0-f8be-4d46-867b-58907c4591ef","timestamp":"2026-08-15T19:41:19.634Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"102d7778-7d44-43c5-b802-8497c2a66780","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/johnroehm/Programming/PythonEnvironments/Physics/Fluid-Based-Physics-Research/SK_EFT_Hawking","sessionId":"6cf37aa2-f1c7-4e33-a712-f17c2d75d99b","version":"2.1.229","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"swift-singing-llama"}

Instead, partial derivatives are passed as explicit\n32\tinput arrays — the algebraic identities they satisfy under\n33\tmetric-compatibility / torsion-freeness are then independent of the\n34\tspecific coordinate setup, which makes the statements naturally\n35\tchart-invariant.\n36\t\n37\t## Scoping mode\n38\t\n39\tAlgebraic-precedent + Mathlib-PR-shape stub. The substantive content\n40\tis in:\n41\t- `riemannQuadraticTerm` — the `ΓΓ − ΓΓ` algebraic part of Riemann\n42\t- `riemannQuadraticTerm_AntisymLastTwo` — substantive antisymmetry\n43\t  in `(μ, ν)` from algebraic structure of the commutator-difference\n44\t- `IsTorsionFree` predicate on Christoffels (= symmetry in lower\n45\t  indices)\n46\t- `christoffelKoszul` — the Koszul-formula closed-form Christoffel\n47\t- `christoffelKoszul_isTorsionFree` — substantive: the Koszul Γ is\n48\t  symmetric in lower indices\n49\t- Bridge to `Curvature.lean`: the Christoffel-quadratic Riemann\n50\t  satisfies the algebraic predicates from 6f.1.\n51\t\n52\tLevi-Civita uniqueness is intentionally deferred to Wave 8 Session 5\n53\t(see §6) — at this scope the substantive content lives in\n54\t`christoffelKoszul`'s closed form rather than a uniqueness theorem;\n55\tthe bundle-level Koszul-bilinear-form uniqueness argument requires\n56\tBonn's `IsCovariantDerivativeOn` API over arbitrary smooth sections.\n57\t\n58\t## What is NOT discharged at this scope (deferred to Mathlib-PR wave)\n59\t\n60\t- The `∂Γ − ∂Γ` part of the full Riemann tensor (requires explicit\n61\t  partial-derivative or chart-coordinate machinery).\n62\t- Algebraic first Bianchi identity for the full Riemann (the\n63\t  commutator-only quadratic part trivially satisfies it; the full\n64\t  Riemann requires the `∂Γ` piece).\n65\t- The full second Bianchi identity `∇_λ R^ρ_{σμν} + ∇_μ R^ρ_{σνλ} +\n66\t  ∇_ν R^ρ_{σλμ} = 0` (requires `∇` on a tensor, which is the next\n67\t  layer of the upstream contribution).\n68\t- Connection-from-bundle (`R(X,Y)Z := ∇_X(∇_Y Z) − ∇_Y(∇_X Z) −\n69\t  ∇_{[X,Y]} Z`) consuming Bonn's bundled `CovariantDerivative` — the\n70\t  full bundle-level proof of antisymmetry-in-(X,Y) requires\n71\t  `IsCovariantDerivativeOn.add` + `mlieBracket` machinery and the\n72\t  same `TensorialAt.mkHom₂` infrastructure as `IsCovariantDerivativeOn.torsion`.\n73\t  This is substantive but multi-session work; module body documents\n74\t  the deferral.\n75\t\n76\t## Anti-pattern audit (per project preemptive-strengthening discipline)\n77\t\n78\t1. **No P1 ∃-absorption:** `christoffelKoszul` is an explicit closed\n79\t   form, not opaque `∃ Γ, ...`.\n80\t2. **No P2 bundle redundancy:** `IsTorsionFree` and the Koszul-formula\n81\t   identity are kept as separate Props.\n82\t3. **No P3 trivial-mult-as-physics:** the antisymmetry of\n83\t   `riemannQuadraticTerm` requires algebraic computation across all\n84\t   four sums, not pure rewriting.\n85\t4. **No P4 vacuous axioms:** `christoffelKoszul_isTorsionFree` requires\n86\t   metric-symmetry hypothesis; the discharge uses it non-trivially.\n87\t5. **No P5 falsifier-restating-hypothesis:** falsifiers not used here;\n88\t   the substantive content is positive-direction identities under\n89\t   load-bearing hypotheses. Levi-Civita uniqueness intentionally\n90\t   deferred to Session 5 (see §6) rather than shipped as a trivial-trans\n91\t   predicate-level uniqueness on a `Γ = christoffelKoszul gInv dg`\n92\t   def-as-equality predicate (which would be the P5 pattern).\n93\t6. **Cross-module bridge integrity P6:** body imports\n94\t   `SKEFTHawking.Curvature` and *calls* `RiemannTensor`, `MetricMatrix`,\n95\t   `sumFin4`, `AntisymLastTwo`. {"parentUuid":"bceea1b2-64f1-4629-b286-632cf95b1075","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"d7b89794-41f0-46fe-9a20-6d198f8f624f","agentId":"a1b16a56a1a83bc81","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01ArCPfLa2kQgbcPb49g7EFK","type":"tool_result","content":"1\timport SKEFTHawking.Basic\n2\timport SKEFTHawking.BundleRiemann\n3\timport Mathlib\n4\t\n5\t/-!\n6\t# Phase 6f Wave 8 Sessions 5 + 5b — Levi-Civita uniqueness at bundle level\n7\t\n8\t## Overview\n9\t\n10\tThe Wave 8 Sessions 5 + 5b deliverable: bundle-level Levi-Civita uniqueness\n11\tvia the Koszul-bilinear-form argument (Wald §3.1 / Kobayashi-Nomizu Thm\n12\tIV.2.2). Every torsion-free metric-compatible connection on the tangent\n13\tbundle of a Riemannian (or non-degenerate Lorentzian) manifold equals the\n14\tKoszul Christoffel — proved as a *substantive* uniqueness theorem (not a\n15\tP5 def-as-equality predicate).\n16\t\n17\t## Wave-headline content\n18\t\n19\t**Session 5 — algebraic uniqueness kernel:**\n20\t\n21\t1. **`IsNondegenerateAt`** — auxiliary predicate: at point `x`, the\n22\t   bilinear form `g x` is non-degenerate (its only null vector is zero).\n23\t2. **`leviCivita_pointwise_unique_of_koszul`** — the algebraic uniqueness\n24\t   kernel: given two connections `cov, cov'` and a non-degenerate bilinear\n25\t   form `g`, if both connections satisfy the same Koszul-form bilinear\n26\t   identity at `x`, then `cov σ x V = cov' σ x V` pointwise. Routes via\n27\t   bilinearity of `g x` and non-degeneracy.\n28\t\n29\t**Session 5b — substantive predicate + Koszul derivation + composition:**\n30\t\n31\t3. **`IsLeviCivita`** — substantive 4-conjunct predicate:\n32\t   `IsCovariantDerivativeOn` + torsion-free + metric-symmetric +\n33\t   metric-compatible (the latter expressed via Mathlib's `extDerivFun`\n34\t   for clean 𝕜-valued exterior derivative of the bilinear-form pairing).\n35\t4. **`koszul_identity_of_isLeviCivita`** — the Wald §3.1 derivation:\n36\t   from `IsLeviCivita cov g`,\n37\t   `2 · g x (cov Y x (X x)) (Z x) = extDerivFun(g(Y,Z))(X) + extDerivFun(g(Z,X))(Y)\n38\t                                    - extDerivFun(g(X,Y))(Z) + g([X,Y],Z)\n39\t                                    - g([X,Z],Y) - g([Y,Z],X)`.\n40\t   Proof via three metric-compat instances at cyclic shifts + three\n41\t   metric-symmetry rewrites (slot 2 → slot 1 alignment) + three\n42\t   torsion-free pairwise substitutions, combined via `linear_combination`.\n43\t5. **`leviCivita_unique_of_isLeviCivita`** — the full uniqueness theorem:\n44\t   two `IsLeviCivita` connections of the same `g` agree pointwise. Routes\n45\t   via Koszul identity + `(2 : 𝕜) ≠ 0` cancellation + the Session 5\n46\t   algebraic kernel.\n47\t\n48\t## Strengthening-discipline note\n49\t\n50\tAn initial Session 5 draft included a 3-conjunct `IsLeviCivita` predicate\n51\twith `metric_symm` standing in for metric-compatibility. End-of-session\n52\treflection caught that `metric_symm` is a property of `g` alone (not the\n53\tcov–g relationship), so the predicate didn't substantively express\n54\t\"cov is Levi-Civita w.r.t. g\". The cut was applied in-session and the\n55\tsubstantive predicate (with `mfderiv`-based metric-compat) shipped in 5b.\n56\t\n57\tPattern logged: predicates with conjuncts that don't constrain the named\n58\trelationship are misleading even if literally substantive — the\n59\tstrengthening check should ask \"does each conjunct constrain a property\n60\tof the named subject (here: cov's relationship to g), or is it a\n61\tside-condition on auxiliary data?\"\n62\t\n63\t## References\n64\t\n65\t- R. M. Wald, *General Relativity* (Univ. Chicago Press, 1984), §3.1\n66\t  Theorem 3.1.1 (Levi-Civita uniqueness via Koszul formula).\n67\t- S. Kobayashi & K. Nomizu, *Foundations of Differential Geometry* Vol. I\n68\t  (Wiley, 1963), Theorem IV.2.2.\n69\t- M. Spivak, *A Comprehensive Introduction to Differential Geometry* Vol. II\n70\t  (Publish or Perish, 1979), Chapter 4 (Koszul formula derivation).\n71\t- P. Massot, M. Rothgang, H. Macbeth, *CovariantDerivative + Torsion*\n72\t  (Mathlib4 2025; pinned `8850ed93`):\n73\t  `IsCovariantDerivativeOn`, `Torsion`, `torsion_eq_zero_iff`.\n74\t\n75\t**First formalization in any proof assistant** (per Phase 6f audit §3E)\n76\tof the bundle-level Levi-Civita uniqueness kernel via the Koszul-bilinear\n77\targument. Mathlib4 has no curvature objects yet (only the Bonn covariant\n78\tderivative API), and no other proof assistant (Coq, Isabelle/AFP, HOL\n79\tLight, HOL4, Mizar, Agda) has Levi-Civita uniqueness.\n80\t-/\n81\t\n82\t@[expose] public section\n83\t\n84\tnamespace SKEFTHawking.LeviCivita\n85\t\n86\topen Bundle NormedSpace\n87\topen VectorField\n88\topen scoped Manifold ContDiff Topology\n89\t\n90\t/-! ## Variable section\n91\t\n92\tWe follow Bonn's `CovariantDerivative.Basic` setup, restricted to the\n93\ttangent bundle (V := TangentSpace I) since Levi-Civita is a structure\n94\ton the tangent connection specifically. -/\n95\t\n96\tvariable {𝕜 : Type*} [NontriviallyNormedField 𝕜]\n97\t  {E : Type*} [NormedAddCommGroup E] [NormedSpace 𝕜 E]\n98\t  {H : Type*} [TopologicalSpace H] {I : ModelWithCorners 𝕜 E H}\n99\t  {M : Type*} [TopologicalSpace M] [ChartedSpace H M]\n100\t  [IsManifold I 1 M] [CompleteSpace E]\n101\t\n102\t/-! ## §1 Non-degeneracy hypothesis\n103\t\n104\tWe state non-degeneracy of `g` at a point as a pointwise predicate on\n105\tthe bilinear form. -/\n106\t\n107\t/-- `g x` is non-degenerate at `x` if its only \"null vector\" is zero:\n108\tfor any `v : TangentSpace I x`, if `g x v w = 0` for all `w`, then\n109\t`v = 0`. -/\n110\tdef IsNondegenerateAt"}]},"uuid":"20137948-79bb-436e-9c16-5c3fd6e7b70a","timestamp":"2026-08-15T19:41:32.682Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"bceea1b2-64f1-4629-b286-632cf95b1075","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/johnroehm/Programming/PythonEnvironments/Physics/Fluid-Based-Physics-Research/SK_EFT_Hawking","sessionId":"6cf37aa2-f1c7-4e33-a712-f17c2d75d99b","version":"2.1.229","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"swift-singing-llama"}
{"parentUuid":"3ce4ad83-8fac-4889-9fce-f1e466404580","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"d7b89794-41f0-46fe-9a20-6d198f8f624f","agentId":"a1b16a56a1a83bc81","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01FGFxfKrg7UupBQ4k8wawTb","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t/-\n2\tCopyright (c) 2026 SK-EFT-Hawking project. All rights reserved.\n3\tReleased under Apache 2.0 license as described in the LICENSE file.\n4\t\n5\tPhase 6g.6 substantive curve-theoretic Wave 9 Session 2 — Mazur σ-model\n6\trigidity at the 1D distillation scope.\n7\t\n8\tDischarges the 6g.6 substantive curve-theoretic gap: Mazur σ-model\n9\trigidity (Mazur 1982 / Bunting / Robinson) at the 1D real-analysis\n10\tdistillation. The substantive content is the **Mazur monotone-rigidity\n11\ttheorem**: a non-decreasing function with matching boundary values is\n12\tconstant on the boundary-bounded interval, plus the **Ernst-potential\n13\tcoincidence corollary**: two Ernst potentials whose difference-squared is\n14\tMazur-rigid coincide on the parametric interval.\n15\t\n16\t**Mathematical content (Mazur 1982 Phys. Lett. A 100, 231–234 / Bunting\n17\t1983 PhD thesis / Robinson 1975 Phys. Rev. Lett. 34, 905–906):** the\n18\tMazur identity for two Ernst potentials `σ_1, σ_2` of vacuum stationary\n19\taxisymmetric solutions:\n20\t\n21\t    ∇·J = ‖∂Σ‖²  ≥  0,\n22\t\n23\twhere `Σ` is the Hermitian-matrix-valued σ-model invariant constructed\n24\tfrom `σ_1, σ_2`. Integrating over the spacetime exterior + applying\n25\tdivergence theorem, the boundary integral (asymptotic flatness +\n26\thorizon + axis) vanishes, hence `‖∂Σ‖² ≡ 0`, hence `Σ` is constant,\n27\thence `σ_1, σ_2` differ by a gauge transformation that is the identity\n28\tunder the shared boundary data. The 1D real-analysis distillation is:\n29\t\n30\t    f' ≥ 0  ∧  f(0) = f(L)  ⟹  f ≡ f(0)  on [0, L].\n31\t\n32\tThe Mazur-energy specialization (`f := (σ_1 - σ_2)²`) reduces Ernst\n33\tcoincidence to monotone-rigidity of the energy.\n34\t\n35\t**Bundle-target alignment:** lifts as **D3 §27** (the no-hair section\n36\tof the correctness-push bundle) per `PAPER_DRAFT_MAPPING.md` Phase 6g\n37\taddendum. Cross-bridge to the existing 6g.6 algebraic-precedent\n38\t`NoHairTheorem.KerrFamilyParams` infrastructure.\n39\t\n40\t**First formalization in any proof assistant** (per Phase 6f audit\n41\t§3E + this session's audit) of the Mazur σ-model rigidity at the 1D\n42\tdistillation scope. Mathlib has\n43\t`monotoneOn_of_deriv_nonneg` and the mean-value-theorem family, but no\n44\tcombined Mazur-Ernst-uniqueness-theorem content; no other proof\n45\tassistant (Coq, Isabelle/AFP, HOL Light, HOL4, Mizar, Agda) has the\n46\tMazur identity in any form per the Phase 6f audit §3E.\n47\t-/\n48\timport SKEFTHawking.NoHairTheorem\n49\timport Mathlib.Analysis.Calculus.Deriv.MeanValue\n50\t\n51\tnamespace SKEFTHawking.MazurSigmaModelRigidity\n52\t\n53\topen Set\n54\topen SKEFTHawking.NoHairTheorem\n55\t\n56\t/-! ## §1 Mazur monotone-rigidity at the 1D distillation\n57\t\n58\tWe encode the curve-level Mazur hypothesis on a parametric interval\n59\t`[0, L]`: a real-valued differentiable function with non-negative\n60\tderivative on the interior. The substantive theorem: matching boundary\n61\tvalues force the function to be constant.\n62\t-/\n63\t\n64\t/--\n65\t**`IsMazurMonotone f L`:** the curve-level Mazur monotonicity\n66\thypothesis on the parametric interval `[0, L]`. The function\n67\t`f : ℝ → ℝ` has explicit derivative `f' : ℝ → ℝ` on the open interior\n68\t`(0, L)` with:\n69\t- `domain_nonneg` — `0 ≤ L` (the interval is non-degenerate);\n70\t- `continuous_on_param` — `f` is continuous on `[0, L]`;\n71\t- `hasDerivAt_interior` — `f` has derivative `f'(λ)` at every\n72\t  `λ ∈ (0, L)`;\n73\t- `deriv_nonneg` — the substantive Mazur σ-model non-negativity:\n74\t  `f'(λ) ≥ 0` on `(0, L)`.\n75\t\n76\tThe `deriv_nonneg` field encodes the Mazur identity content `∇·J =\n77\t‖∂Σ‖² ≥ 0` at the curve-level pullback.\n78\t-/\n79\tstructure IsMazurMonotone (f f' : ℝ → ℝ) (L : ℝ) : Prop where\n80\t  /-- The parametric interval `[0, L]` is non-degenerate. -/\n81\t  domain_nonneg : (0 : ℝ) ≤ L\n82\t  /-- Continuity on the closed parametric interval. -/\n83\t  continuous_on_param : ContinuousOn f (Set.Icc (0 : ℝ) L)\n84\t  /-- Differentiability + explicit derivative on the open interior. -/\n85\t  hasDerivAt_interior : ∀ lam ∈ Set.Ioo (0 : ℝ) L, HasDerivAt f (f' lam) lam\n86\t  /-- The substantive Mazur identity: `f'(λ) ≥ 0` on `(0, L)`. -/\n87\t  deriv_nonneg : ∀ lam ∈ Set.Ioo (0 : ℝ) L, 0 ≤ f' lam\n88\t\n89\t/-! ## §2 Wave-headline Mazur monotone-rigidity theorem\n90\t\n91\tThe substantive Mazur monotone-rigidity: under `IsMazurMonotone f L`\n92\tplus matching boundary values `f(0) = f(L)`, conclude `f` is constant\n93\ton `[0, L]`.\n94\t-/\n95\t\n96\t/--\n97\t**Mazur monotone-rigidity theorem (Wave 9 Session 2 headline).**\n98\tUnder the curve-level Mazur hypothesis `IsMazurMonotone f f' L` plus\n99\tmatching boundary values `f(0) = f(L)`, the function `f` is constant\n100\ton `[0, L]`.\n101\t\n102\t**Substantive content:** this is the 1D real-analysis distillation of\n103\tthe Mazur σ-model rigidity argument. The non-negativity of the\n104\tderivative (Mazur identity content) plus matching boundary data\n105\t(asymptotic flatness + horizon + axis vanishing) forces the σ-model\n106\tenergy to be constant, hence vanishing in the relevant gauge.\n107\t\n108\t**Proof:** apply `monotoneOn_of_deriv_nonneg` on the convex set\n109\t`Icc 0 L` to obtain `MonotoneOn f (Icc 0 L)`. Then for `λ ∈ Icc 0 L`,\n110\tsandwich `f(0) ≤ f(λ) ≤ f(L) = f(0)` gives `f(λ) = f(0)`."}]},"uuid":"d641f487-e0cd-4b58-b9eb-9027a8e5be77","timestamp":"2026-08-15T19:41:32.685Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"3ce4ad83-8fac-4889-9fce-f1e466404580","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/johnroehm/Programming/PythonEnvironments/Physics/Fluid-Based-Physics-Research/SK_EFT_Hawking","sessionId":"6cf37aa2-f1c7-4e33-a712-f17c2d75d99b","version":"2.1.229","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"swift-singing-llama"}

{"parentUuid":"70a428e9-bb60-4ac5-9c33-6c00e8b96dea","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"d7b89794-41f0-46fe-9a20-6d198f8f624f","agentId":"a1b16a56a1a83bc81","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01HtqGoLhygduSnkseUqgwpK","type":"tool_result","content":"126:def sumFin3 (f : Fin 3 → ℝ) : ℝ :=\n143:def lowerShift (γ : SpatialMetric) (N_shift : Vec3) (i : Fin 3) : ℝ :=\n148:noncomputable def admFourMetric_00\n154:def admFourMetric_0i\n165:theorem minkowski_admFourMetric_00 :\n178:theorem minkowski_admFourMetric_0i (i : Fin 3) :\n192:def extrinsicCurvatureTrace\n200:def extrinsicCurvatureSquared\n213:noncomputable def hamiltonianConstraint\n228:theorem hamiltonianConstraint_vacuum_iff\n252:theorem hamiltonianConstraint_moment_of_time_symmetry\n268:theorem hamiltonianConstraint_moment_of_time_symmetry_iff\n286:noncomputable def momentumConstraint_i\n300:theorem momentumConstraint_vacuum_iff\n312:def minkowskiADMData : ℝ × ℝ × ℝ × ℝ := (0, 0, 0, 0)\n319:theorem minkowski_satisfies_hamiltonianConstraint :\n328:theorem minkowski_satisfies_momentumConstraint :\n355:theorem deSitter_flat_slicing_hamiltonian_iff\n395:def schwarzschildADMMass (M : ℝ) : ℝ := M\n410:theorem schwarzschild_adm_mass_eq_half_horizon_radius (M : ℝ) :\n427:def deSitterADMMass : ℝ := 0\n526:theorem _phase6f_w5_module_summary_marker : True := trivial"}]},"uuid":"ec64d739-476d-47cd-af7e-484d3c9355f2","timestamp":"2026-08-15T19:41:42.129Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"70a428e9-bb60-4ac5-9c33-6c00e8b96dea","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/johnroehm/Programming/PythonEnvironments/Physics/Fluid-Based-Physics-Research/SK_EFT_Hawking","sessionId":"6cf37aa2-f1c7-4e33-a712-f17c2d75d99b","version":"2.1.229","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"swift-singing-llama"}

{"parentUuid":"a3fd79d7-91e6-473c-933f-130753d7bd19","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a1b16a56a1a83bc81","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011Ce56yegDXCh7j1mizkE7w","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_0136vmYg72h2MgwcXJFthBo2","name":"Read","input":{"file_path":"/Users/johnroehm/Programming/PythonEnvironments/Physics/Fluid-Based-Physics-Research/SK_EFT_Hawking/.claude/worktrees/redraft-D3/lean/SKEFTHawking/TetradFormalism.lean","offset":100,"limit":190},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":2189,"cache_read_input_tokens":163013,"output_tokens":276,"output_tokens_details":{"thinking_tokens":92},"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":2189},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":276,"cache_read_input_tokens":163013,"cache_creation_input_tokens":2189,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":2189,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011Ce56ydtb8YdTHBrqq5yGb","attributionAgent":"skeft-qa:paper-drafter","attributionSkill":"skeft-qa:paper-authoring","attributionPlugin":"skeft-qa","type":"assistant","uuid":"f925a5aa-03b2-47e2-8f4b-308a9e5d499a","timestamp":"2026-08-15T19:41:47.420Z","effort":"high","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/johnroehm/Programming/PythonEnvironments/Physics/Fluid-Based-Physics-Research/SK_EFT_Hawking","sessionId":"6cf37aa2-f1c7-4e33-a712-f17c2d75d99b","version":"2.1.229","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"swift-singing-llama"}
{"parentUuid":"f925a5aa-03b2-47e2-8f4b-308a9e5d499a","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"d7b89794-41f0-46fe-9a20-6d198f8f624f","agentId":"a1b16a56a1a83bc81","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_0136vmYg72h2MgwcXJFthBo2","type":"tool_result","content":"100\t\n101\t/-- **Inverse tetrad** `E_a^μ` as a 4×4 matrix. Satisfies\n102\t    `e^a_μ E_b^μ = δ^a_b` (for `a, b ∈ Fin 4`). -/\n103\tabbrev InverseTetrad : Type := Fin 4 → Fin 4 → ℝ\n104\t\n105\t/-! ## §2 — Tetrad-metric equivalence\n106\t\n107\tThe metric is recovered from the tetrad via\n108\t    g_μν = η_{ab} e^a_μ e^b_ν\n109\t\n110\twith η_{ab} the Minkowski metric in the orthonormal frame.\n111\t-/\n112\t\n113\t/-- **Tetrad-induced metric**: `g_μν := η_{ab} e^a_μ e^b_ν`.\n114\t\n115\tThis is the load-bearing tetrad-metric equivalence: every tetrad\n116\tconfiguration `e` defines a unique 4-metric via index contraction\n117\tthrough the flat-frame Minkowski metric η. -/\n118\tdef tetradInducedMetric (e : Tetrad) (μ ν : Fin 4) : ℝ :=\n119\t  sumFin4 (fun a => sumFin4 (fun b => η a b * e a μ * e b ν))\n120\t\n121\t/-- **Tetrad-induced metric is symmetric**: `g_μν = g_νμ`.\n122\t    Substantive: the tetrad construction yields a symmetric metric\n123\t    automatically (since η is symmetric). This is the algebraic\n124\t    basis for \"tetrad GR is consistent with metric GR\" before\n125\t    even invoking the Cartan structure equations.\n126\t-/\n127\ttheorem tetradInducedMetric_symm (e : Tetrad) (μ ν : Fin 4) :\n128\t    tetradInducedMetric e μ ν = tetradInducedMetric e ν μ := by\n129\t  unfold tetradInducedMetric sumFin4\n130\t  -- η is diagonal in the Lorentz frame; substitute all 16 values\n131\t  have h00 : η 0 0 = -1 := η_zero_zero\n132\t  have h11 : η 1 1 = 1 := η_spatial_diag 1 (by decide)\n133\t  have h22 : η 2 2 = 1 := η_spatial_diag 2 (by decide)\n134\t  have h33 : η 3 3 = 1 := η_spatial_diag 3 (by decide)\n135\t  have h01 : η 0 1 = 0 := η_off_diag 0 1 (by decide)\n136\t  have h02 : η 0 2 = 0 := η_off_diag 0 2 (by decide)\n137\t  have h03 : η 0 3 = 0 := η_off_diag 0 3 (by decide)\n138\t  have h10 : η 1 0 = 0 := η_off_diag 1 0 (by decide)\n139\t  have h12 : η 1 2 = 0 := η_off_diag 1 2 (by decide)\n140\t  have h13 : η 1 3 = 0 := η_off_diag 1 3 (by decide)\n141\t  have h20 : η 2 0 = 0 := η_off_diag 2 0 (by decide)\n142\t  have h21 : η 2 1 = 0 := η_off_diag 2 1 (by decide)\n143\t  have h23 : η 2 3 = 0 := η_off_diag 2 3 (by decide)\n144\t  have h30 : η 3 0 = 0 := η_off_diag 3 0 (by decide)\n145\t  have h31 : η 3 1 = 0 := η_off_diag 3 1 (by decide)\n146\t  have h32 : η 3 2 = 0 := η_off_diag 3 2 (by decide)\n147\t  simp only [h00, h11, h22, h33, h01, h02, h03, h10, h12, h13, h20, h21,\n148\t             h23, h30, h31, h32]\n149\t  ring\n150\t\n151\t/-! ## §3 — Minkowski tetrad\n152\t\n153\tThe canonical Minkowski tetrad `e^a_μ = δ^a_μ` (Kronecker delta)\n154\trecovers the Minkowski metric `g_μν = η_μν` exactly.\n155\t-/\n156\t\n157\t/-- **Minkowski (Lorentz-frame) tetrad**: `e^a_μ = δ^a_μ`. The\n158\t    canonical orthonormal frame at any point of Minkowski space. -/\n159\tdef minkowskiTetrad : Tetrad :=\n160\t  fun a μ => kron a μ\n161\t\n162\t/--\n163\t**Minkowski tetrad induces the Minkowski metric.** Substantive\n164\tnamed identity: the canonical Minkowski tetrad recovers the\n165\tflat metric η via the tetrad-metric equivalence.\n166\t\n167\tProof: g_μν = Σ_{ab} η_{ab} δ^a_μ δ^b_ν = η_μν directly.\n168\t\n169\tThis is the **load-bearing consistency check** of the tetrad\n170\tformalism: in the trivial Lorentz frame, tetrad GR reduces to\n171\tmetric GR with η as the metric.\n172\t-/\n173\ttheorem minkowskiTetrad_induces_minkowski_metric (μ ν : Fin 4) :\n174\t    tetradInducedMetric minkowskiTetrad μ ν = η μ ν := by\n175\t  unfold tetradInducedMetric minkowskiTetrad kron sumFin4\n176\t  -- Σ_{ab} η_ab · δ^a_μ · δ^b_ν = η_μν\n177\t  fin_cases μ <;> fin_cases ν <;> simp\n178\t\n179\t/-! ## §4 — Tetrad determinant and orthonormality -/\n180\t\n181\t/-- **Tetrad determinant** `det(e) = e^0_0 e^1_1 e^2_2 e^3_3 - ...`\n182\t    For diagonal tetrads (Minkowski-frame), this reduces to the\n183\t    product of diagonal entries. We define the **diagonal-tetrad\n184\t    determinant** as a substantive named quantity.\n185\t\n186\tFor the Minkowski tetrad `e^a_μ = δ^a_μ`, det(e) = 1.\n187\t\n188\tNote: the full 4×4 determinant requires more involved formula;\n189\twe ship the diagonal specialization here as the load-bearing\n190\tnamed quantity for downstream consumers. -/\n191\tdef diagonalTetradDet (e : Tetrad) : ℝ := e 0 0 * e 1 1 * e 2 2 * e 3 3\n192\t\n193\t/--\n194\t**Minkowski tetrad determinant equals 1.** Substantive named\n195\tidentity: the canonical Minkowski tetrad has unit determinant,\n196\tconfirming it's a proper orthonormal frame.\n197\t\n198\tCross-bridge: for non-trivial spacetimes, the tetrad determinant\n199\tencodes the volume element; det(e) = √|det(g)| (Cartan).\n200\t-/\n201\ttheorem minkowskiTetrad_det_eq_one :\n202\t    diagonalTetradDet minkowskiTetrad = 1 := by\n203\t  unfold diagonalTetradDet minkowskiTetrad kron\n204\t  simp\n205\t\n206\t/-! ## §5 — Torsion structure (algebraic level)\n207\t\n208\tThe torsion 2-form T^a satisfies the first Cartan structure\n209\tequation T^a = de^a + ω^a_b ∧ e^b. The torsion-free condition\n210\tT^a = 0 recovers the Levi-Civita connection.\n211\t\n212\tWe encode the **torsion residual** as an external scalar\n213\tparameter (the d and ∧ machinery requires differential forms);\n214\tthe substantive content is the torsion-vanishing biconditional\n215\tand the cross-bridge to Phase 6e.6 EinsteinCartanExtension.\n216\t-/\n217\t\n218\t/-- **Torsion residual** at the algebraic level: scalar parameter\n219\t    encoding the deviation from the Levi-Civita connection. The\n220\t    full Cartan-structure-equation T^a = de^a + ω^a_b ∧ e^b is\n221\t    deferred to a future wave with differential-form machinery. -/\n222\tdef torsionResidual (T_amplitude : ℝ) : ℝ := T_amplitude\n223\t\n224\t\n225\t/-! ## §6 — Cross-bridge to Phase 6e.6 Einstein-Cartan\n226\t\n227\tPhase 6e.6 ships the Einstein-Cartan torsion amplitude\n228\t`torsionAmplitude (Λ_UV N_f α_EC n_spin)` and the biconditional\n229\t`ecResidual_eq_zero_iff_alpha_unity` which states that the\n230\tEC residual vanishes iff α_EC = 1 (the metric-formalism case).\n231\t\n232\tThe 6f.6 cross-bridge: when the Einstein-Cartan torsion amplitude\n233\tsatisfies α_EC = 1 (so EC residual = 0), the tetrad formalism\n234\treduces to the metric formalism (torsion-free Levi-Civita).\n235\t-/\n236\t\n237\t/--\n238\t**Tetrad-metric formalism equivalence at α_EC = 1.** Substantive\n239\tcross-bridge to Phase 6e.6: when the Einstein-Cartan parameter\n240\tα_EC = 1, the EC residual vanishes (Lean\n241\t`ecResidual_eq_zero_iff_alpha_unity`), and equivalently the\n242\ttetrad formalism reduces to torsion-free Levi-Civita (so\n243\ttetrad EFE = metric EFE).\n244\t\n245\tThis is the **load-bearing tetrad-vs-metric formalism equivalence\n246\ttheorem** (Wald 1984 §3.4 \"Tetrad formalism\" footnote: the two\n247\tformalisms are equivalent when torsion = 0).\n248\t\n249\tCross-bridge: this theorem consumes Phase 6e.6's\n250\t`SKEFTHawking.EinsteinCartanExtension.ecResidual_eq_zero_iff_alpha_unity`\n251\tby name, making the bridge between the two formalisms explicit.\n252\t-/\n253\ttheorem tetrad_metric_equivalence_at_alpha_one\n254\t    (Λ_UV N_f n_spin : ℝ) :\n255\t    SKEFTHawking.EinsteinCartanExtension.ecResidual Λ_UV N_f 1 n_spin = 0 :=\n256\t  SKEFTHawking.EinsteinCartanExtension.ecResidual_at_alpha_one\n257\t    Λ_UV N_f n_spin\n258\t\n259\t/--\n260\t**Tetrad formalism Levi-Civita reduction biconditional.** The\n261\ttetrad EC residual vanishes iff α_EC = 1. Specialization of\n262\tPhase 6e.6's biconditional with positive Λ_UV, N_f, n_spin.\n263\t\n264\tSubstantive: makes the α_EC = 1 ↔ Levi-Civita-tetrad equivalence\n265\texplicit at the named-API level for downstream consumers.\n266\t-/\n267\ttheorem tetrad_levi_civita_iff_alpha_unity\n268\t    {Λ_UV N_f α_EC n_spin : ℝ}\n269\t    (hΛ : 0 < Λ_UV) (hN : 0 < N_f) (hn : n_spin ≠ 0) :\n270\t    SKEFTHawking.EinsteinCartanExtension.ecResidual Λ_UV N_f α_EC n_spin = 0\n271\t      ↔ α_EC = 1 :=\n272\t  SKEFTHawking.EinsteinCartanExtension.ecResidual_eq_zero_iff_alpha_unity\n273\t    hΛ hN hn\n274\t\n275\t/-! ## §7 — Module summary -/\n276\t\n277\t/--\n278\t**Phase 6f Wave 6 module summary marker.** This module ships the\n279\ttetrad (vierbein) formulation of general relativity at the\n280\talgebraic / point-wise level, **closing Phase 6f**.\n281\t\n282\t**Theorem roster** (5 substantive theorems + 1 marker):\n283\t\n284\t§2 Tetrad-metric equivalence:\n285\t- `tetradInducedMetric_symm` — tetrad-induced metric is symmetric\n286\t  (load-bearing consistency check)\n287\t\n288\t§3 Minkowski tetrad:\n289\t- `minkowskiTetrad_induces_minkowski_metric` — `g = η` from"}]},"uuid":"6a7757af-8015-4227-ba24-d74411792a3e","timestamp":"2026-08-15T19:41:47.496Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"f925a5aa-03b2-47e2-8f4b-308a9e5d499a","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/johnroehm/Programming/PythonEnvironments/Physics/Fluid-Based-Physics-Research/SK_EFT_Hawking","sessionId":"6cf37aa2-f1c7-4e33-a712-f17c2d75d99b","version":"2.1.229","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"swift-singing-llama"}
