{"parentUuid":"9d1bb024-b294-4bb1-888f-d9ba8000bbec","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a1b16a56a1a83bc81","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011Ce56z1RdniDgiPJJZNafr","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01K5FxZdBzsjWnQAD9GzXtG6","name":"Grep","input":{"pattern":"^(theorem|lemma|def|structure|noncomputable def)\\s+[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_']*","path":"/Users/johnroehm/Programming/PythonEnvironments/Physics/Fluid-Based-Physics-Research/SK_EFT_Hawking/.claude/worktrees/redraft-D3/lean/SKEFTHawking/EinsteinTensor.lean","output_mode":"content","-n":true},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":4591,"cache_read_input_tokens":165202,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":4591,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011Ce56yzanX2N1KhAr4A6Dy","attributionAgent":"skeft-qa:paper-drafter","attributionSkill":"skeft-qa:paper-authoring","attributionPlugin":"skeft-qa","type":"assistant","uuid":"358993eb-fc60-480e-a7a2-66fd57df837f","timestamp":"2026-08-15T19:41:56.849Z","effort":"high","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/johnroehm/Programming/PythonEnvironments/Physics/Fluid-Based-Physics-Research/SK_EFT_Hawking","sessionId":"6cf37aa2-f1c7-4e33-a712-f17c2d75d99b","version":"2.1.229","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"swift-singing-llama"}
{"parentUuid":"9d1bb024-b294-4bb1-888f-d9ba8000bbec","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"d7b89794-41f0-46fe-9a20-6d198f8f624f","agentId":"a1b16a56a1a83bc81","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01WHmpGBvQt1kdWZFB7dSDvW","type":"tool_result","content":"205\t\n206\t/-! ## §4 — Hamiltonian constraint -/\n207\t\n208\t/-- **Hamiltonian constraint** (Wald 1984 Eq. 10.2.28):\n209\t\n210\t    H := ^(3)R + (tr K)² - K_{ij} K^{ij} - 16π G ρ\n211\t\n212\tFor G = 1 (geometric units), this is the canonical form. -/\n213\tnoncomputable def hamiltonianConstraint\n214\t    (R3 trK Ksq ρ : ℝ) : ℝ :=\n215\t  R3 + trK ^ 2 - Ksq - 16 * Real.pi * ρ\n216\t\n217\t/--\n218\t**Hamiltonian constraint vacuum biconditional** (substantive\n219\tbiconditional):\n220\t\n221\tFor ρ = 0 (vacuum), `H = 0 ↔ ^(3)R + (tr K)² = K_{ij} K^{ij}`.\n222\t\n223\tThis is the **load-bearing geometric content of vacuum ADM\n224\tdata**: the spatial scalar curvature plus the squared mean\n225\tcurvature equals the squared trace-free extrinsic curvature.\n226\tWald 1984 §10.2.\n227\t-/\n228\ttheorem hamiltonianConstraint_vacuum_iff\n229\t    (R3 trK Ksq : ℝ) :\n230\t    hamiltonianConstraint R3 trK Ksq 0 = 0 ↔ R3 + trK ^ 2 = Ksq := by\n231\t  unfold hamiltonianConstraint\n232\t  constructor\n233\t  · intro h\n234\t    linarith\n235\t  · intro h\n236\t    linarith\n237\t\n238\t/--\n239\t**Moment-of-time-symmetry Hamiltonian constraint** (`K = 0`\n240\tspecialization):\n241\t\n242\tWhen `K_{ij} = 0` (moment-of-time-symmetry slicing), tr K = 0\n243\tand K_{ij} K^{ij} = 0, so\n244\t\n245\t    H = ^(3)R - 16π G ρ.\n246\t\n247\tThis reduces to the Yamabe-form scalar-curvature equation:\n248\t`^(3)R = 16π G ρ` for ρ ≥ 0 (positive-energy condition). Used\n249\tin Schwarzschild's t = const slicing (where K = 0 — see\n250\t`schwarzschild_adm_K_zero` below).\n251\t-/\n252\ttheorem hamiltonianConstraint_moment_of_time_symmetry\n253\t    (R3 ρ : ℝ) :\n254\t    hamiltonianConstraint R3 0 0 ρ = R3 - 16 * Real.pi * ρ := by\n255\t  unfold hamiltonianConstraint\n256\t  ring\n257\t\n258\t/--\n259\t**Yamabe-positive scalar curvature biconditional at moment-of-\n260\ttime-symmetry.** For `K = 0` and `ρ ≥ 0`, the Hamiltonian\n261\tconstraint H = 0 holds iff `^(3)R = 16π G ρ ≥ 0`.\n262\t\n263\tThe substantive content: at moment-of-time-symmetry the spatial\n264\tslice is **Yamabe-positive** when matter density is non-negative.\n265\tThis is the algebraic basis for the positive-energy theorem on\n266\tmoment-of-time-symmetry slices.\n267\t-/\n268\ttheorem hamiltonianConstraint_moment_of_time_symmetry_iff\n269\t    (R3 ρ : ℝ) :\n270\t    hamiltonianConstraint R3 0 0 ρ = 0 ↔ R3 = 16 * Real.pi * ρ := by\n271\t  unfold hamiltonianConstraint\n272\t  constructor\n273\t  · intro h; linarith\n274\t  · intro h; linarith\n275\t\n276\t/-! ## §5 — Momentum constraint -/\n277\t\n278\t/-- **Momentum constraint** (Wald 1984 Eq. 10.2.29):\n279\t\n280\t    M^i := D_j (K^{ij} - γ^{ij} K) - 8π G j^i\n281\t\n282\tFor G = 1. We supply the divergence\n283\t`divKtraceFree^i := D_j (K^{ij} - γ^{ij} K)` as an external\n284\tinput (since computing it from K requires spatial covariant\n285\tderivatives D_j beyond the project's algebraic level). -/\n286\tnoncomputable def momentumConstraint_i\n287\t    (divKtraceFree_i ji : ℝ) : ℝ :=\n288\t  divKtraceFree_i - 8 * Real.pi * ji\n289\t\n290\t/--\n291\t**Momentum constraint vacuum biconditional.** For `j^i = 0`\n292\t(vacuum), the momentum constraint M^i = 0 holds iff the\n293\ttrace-free divergence vanishes: `D_j (K^{ij} - γ^{ij} K) = 0`.\n294\t\n295\tThe substantive content: vacuum slicings have\n296\t**divergence-free trace-free extrinsic curvature**. This is\n297\tthe algebraic basis for Lichnerowicz's CMC (constant mean\n298\tcurvature) decomposition of ADM initial data.\n299\t-/\n300\ttheorem momentumConstraint_vacuum_iff\n301\t    (divKtraceFree_i : ℝ) :\n302\t    momentumConstraint_i divKtraceFree_i 0 = 0 ↔ divKtraceFree_i = 0 := by\n303\t  unfold momentumConstraint_i\n304\t  constructor\n305\t  · intro h; linarith\n306\t  · intro h; linarith\n307\t\n308\t/-! ## §6 — Specific spacetime ADM data -/\n309\t\n310\t/-- **Minkowski ADM data**: `R3 = 0`, `tr K = 0`, `K_{ij} K^{ij} = 0`,\n311\t    `ρ = 0`. The trivial vacuum ADM data. -/\n312\tdef minkowskiADMData : ℝ × ℝ × ℝ × ℝ := (0, 0, 0, 0)\n313\t\n314\t/--\n315\t**Minkowski satisfies the Hamiltonian constraint.** For Minkowski\n316\tADM data (all zeros), H = 0 + 0 - 0 - 0 = 0. Substantive:\n317\tconfirms Minkowski is a valid vacuum ADM initial data set.\n318\t-/\n319\ttheorem minkowski_satisfies_hamiltonianConstraint :\n320\t    hamiltonianConstraint 0 0 0 0 = 0 := by\n321\t  unfold hamiltonianConstraint\n322\t  simp\n323\t\n324\t/--\n325\t**Minkowski satisfies the momentum constraint** at every spatial\n326\tdirection. {"parentUuid":"f4939900-93a4-4e70-afd0-527a4aeb771d","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a1b16a56a1a83bc81","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011Ce57328dp3cVo3ccjv8kh","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"I found `leviCivita_unique_for_lorentzianBundle` — exactly the indefinite-signature Levi-Civita uniqueness result I needed, with `IsLorentzianBundle` carrying symmetry and nondegeneracy but no positive-definiteness requirement. {"parentUuid":"bac90b78-e1b2-45a7-aba1-08b2599ba8f9","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"d7b89794-41f0-46fe-9a20-6d198f8f624f","agentId":"a1b16a56a1a83bc81","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_018Zz3Z6rY6JBwLCEh5P41rK","type":"tool_result","content":"122-\n123-variable {𝕜 : Type*} [NontriviallyNormedField 𝕜]\n124-  {E : Type*} [NormedAddCommGroup E] [NormedSpace 𝕜 E]\n125-  {H : Type*} [TopologicalSpace H] {I : ModelWithCorners 𝕜 E H}\n126-  {M : Type*} [TopologicalSpace M] [ChartedSpace H M]\n127-  [IsManifold I 1 M] [CompleteSpace E]\n128-\n129-/-! ## §1 Bundle-level Lorentzian metric predicate -/\n130-\n131-/--\n132-**`IsLorentzianBundle g`:** the family of fiber bilinear forms\n133-`g : Π x : M, TangentSpace I x →L[𝕜] TangentSpace I x →L[𝕜] 𝕜`\n134-constitutes a Lorentzian metric on the tangent bundle if it is\n135-*symmetric* at every fiber and *non-degenerate* at every fiber.\n136-\n137-The signature condition (existence of an orthonormal frame putting\n138-`g x` into `(-, +, +, ..., +)` form) is independent of these two\n139-structural conditions and is captured at the algebraic-precedent level\n140-in `LorentzianMetric.IsLorentzianSignature`. The bundle-level layer\n141-here ships the symmetry-and-non-degeneracy backbone, which is what the\n142-bundle-level Levi-Civita uniqueness derivation (Wave 8 Sessions 5+5b)\n143-consumes.\n144-\n145-The choice to bundle symmetry + non-degeneracy at the bundle level\n146-(rather than full Lorentzian signature) follows O'Neill's semi-Riemannian\n147-treatment: the Levi-Civita connection exists and is unique on any\n148-non-degenerate symmetric bilinear form, regardless of signature.\n149-Lorentzian and Riemannian metrics are then specializations distinguished\n150-by signature.\n151--/\n152:structure IsLorentzianBundle\n153-    (g : Π x : M, TangentSpace I x →L[𝕜] TangentSpace I x →L[𝕜] 𝕜) : Prop where\n154-  /-- `g x` is symmetric at every point. -/\n155-  symm : ∀ (x : M) (v w : TangentSpace I x), g x v w = g x w v\n156-  /-- `g x` is non-degenerate at every point: only zero vector pairs\n157-  trivially against everything. -/\n158-  nondegenerate : ∀ x : M, IsNondegenerateAt g x\n159-\n160-/-! ## §2 Direct projection theorems\n161-\n162-These are the structural projections feeding into the LeviCivita /\n163-RaychaudhuriEquation pipeline downstream. They look thin in isolation\n164-but each is a load-bearing API endpoint: the named theorems can be\n165-called by downstream consumers without exposing the structure\n166-constructor pattern. -/\n167-\n168-omit [IsManifold I 1 M] [CompleteSpace E] in\n169-/--\n170-**`lorentzianBundle_isNondegenerateAt`:** every fiber of a Lorentzian\n171-bundle is non-degenerate. This is the direct consumer of\n172-`LeviCivita.leviCivita_pointwise_unique_of_koszul` — the algebraic\n173-uniqueness kernel ingests `IsNondegenerateAt g x` as its non-degeneracy\n174-hypothesis. -/\n175-theorem lorentzianBundle_isNondegenerateAt\n176-    {g : Π x : M, TangentSpace I x →L[𝕜] TangentSpace I x →L[𝕜] 𝕜}\n177-    (h : IsLorentzianBundle g) (x : M) :"}]},"uuid":"f2fffdcc-0227-41a7-a830-c5f25db481ec","timestamp":"2026-08-15T19:42:38.045Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"bac90b78-e1b2-45a7-aba1-08b2599ba8f9","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/johnroehm/Programming/PythonEnvironments/Physics/Fluid-Based-Physics-Research/SK_EFT_Hawking","sessionId":"6cf37aa2-f1c7-4e33-a712-f17c2d75d99b","version":"2.1.229","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"swift-singing-llama"}

Two real-valued\n154\tErnst-potential functions `σ_1, σ_2 : ℝ → ℝ` satisfy:\n155\t- the Mazur energy `e := (σ_1 − σ_2)²` is `IsMazurMonotone` with the\n156\t  explicit derivative `e' : ℝ → ℝ` (encoding the σ-model\n157\t  non-negativity);\n158\t- the boundary values match: `σ_1(0) = σ_2(0)` (asymptotic / inner)\n159\t  and `σ_1(L) = σ_2(L)` (asymptotic / outer).\n160\t\n161\tThe substantive content is the encoded σ-model non-negativity in\n162\t`IsMazurMonotone` plus the boundary-value matching.\n163\t-/\n164\tstructure IsErnstPotentialPair (σ_1 σ_2 e' : ℝ → ℝ) (L : ℝ) : Prop where\n165\t  /-- The Mazur energy `e := (σ_1 − σ_2)²` satisfies\n166\t  `IsMazurMonotone`. -/\n167\t  energy_mazur : IsMazurMonotone (fun lam => (σ_1 lam - σ_2 lam)^2) e' L\n168\t  /-- Boundary matching at `λ = 0`. -/\n169\t  match_zero : σ_1 0 = σ_2 0\n170\t  /-- Boundary matching at `λ = L`. -/\n171\t  match_L : σ_1 L = σ_2 L\n172\t\n173\t/--\n174\t**Ernst-potential coincidence corollary (Wave 9 Session 2 substantive\n175\tconsequence).** Under `IsErnstPotentialPair σ_1 σ_2 e' L`, the two\n176\tErnst potentials coincide on the parametric interval `[0, L]`.\n177\t\n178\t**Substantive content:** the σ-model energy `(σ_1 − σ_2)²` vanishes at\n179\tboth boundaries (matching boundary data) and is `IsMazurMonotone`\n180\t(Mazur identity content). By §2's `mazur_monotone_rigidity`, the\n181\tenergy is constant on `[0, L]`, hence equals its boundary value `0`.\n182\tA non-negative quantity equal to zero forces the underlying difference\n183\tto vanish, hence the two potentials coincide.\n184\t\n185\tThis is the curve-theoretic 1D distillation of the no-hair theorem's\n186\tMazur argument: matching boundary data forces uniqueness.\n187\t-/\n188\ttheorem ernst_potential_coincidence\n189\t    {σ_1 σ_2 e' : ℝ → ℝ} {L : ℝ}\n190\t    (h : IsErnstPotentialPair σ_1 σ_2 e' L) :\n191\t    ∀ lam ∈ Set.Icc (0 : ℝ) L, σ_1 lam = σ_2 lam := by\n192\t  -- The Mazur energy at the boundary vanishes:\n193\t  have h_e_zero : (σ_1 0 - σ_2 0)^2 = 0 := by\n194\t    rw [h.match_zero]; ring\n195\t  have h_e_L : (σ_1 L - σ_2 L)^2 = 0 := by\n196\t    rw [h.match_L]; ring\n197\t  -- Boundary matching of the energy: e(0) = 0 = e(L), so e(0) = e(L)\n198\t  have h_e_boundary : (σ_1 0 - σ_2 0)^2 = (σ_1 L - σ_2 L)^2 := by\n199\t    rw [h_e_zero, h_e_L]\n200\t  -- Apply Mazur monotone-rigidity to the energy\n201\t  have h_e_const :=\n202\t    mazur_monotone_rigidity h.energy_mazur h_e_boundary\n203\t  -- For any λ ∈ Icc 0 L, e(λ) = e(0) = 0\n204\t  intro lam hlam\n205\t  have h_e_lam : (σ_1 lam - σ_2 lam)^2 = (σ_1 0 - σ_2 0)^2 := h_e_const lam hlam\n206\t  rw [h_e_zero] at h_e_lam\n207\t  -- (σ_1 lam - σ_2 lam)^2 = 0 ⟹ σ_1 lam - σ_2 lam = 0 ⟹ σ_1 lam = σ_2 lam\n208\t  have h_diff_zero : σ_1 lam - σ_2 lam = 0 := by\n209\t    have := sq_eq_zero_iff.mp h_e_lam\n210\t    exact this\n211\t  linarith\n212\t\n213\t/-! ## §4 Cross-bridge to existing 6g.6 NoHairTheorem (KerrFamilyParams)\n214\t\n215\tThe substantive cross-bridge: two Kerr family members with matching\n216\tmass + matching angular momentum coincide as Kerr-family parameters.\n217\tThis is the algebraic-precedent Mazur rigidity at the parameter level,\n218\tspecialized from §3's curve-level Ernst coincidence.\n219\t-/\n220\t\n221\t/--\n222\t**Substantive cross-bridge: KerrFamilyParams determined by `(M, J)`.**\n223\tTwo `KerrFamilyParams` instances `k_1, k_2` with matching mass and\n224\tmatching angular momentum have the same parametric data — the Mazur\n225\tσ-model rigidity at the parameter-level.\n226\t\n227\t**Substantive content:** consumes both `k.M` and `k.J` projections + a\n228\t`KerrFamilyParams.mk` reconstruction. While the proof is a structural\n229\textensionality argument, the *content* is the Mazur uniqueness\n230\tstatement at the algebraic-precedent scope: the two-parameter family\n231\t`(M, J)` exhausts the moduli space of vacuum stationary axisymmetric\n232\tblack holes (as encoded by `KerrFamilyParams`).\n233\t\n234\tThe cross-bridge to §3's curve-level Ernst coincidence: if the two\n235\tKerr-family Ernst potentials are paired with matching boundary values,\n236\t§3 gives `σ_1 = σ_2` on the parametric interval, and the\n237\tKerrFamilyParams reconstruction shows this forces parameter equality.\n238\t-/\n239\ttheorem kerrFamilyParams_unique_from_M_J\n240\t    {k_1 k_2 : KerrFamilyParams}\n241\t    (h_M : k_1.M = k_2.M) (h_J : k_1.J = k_2.J) :\n242\t    k_1 = k_2 := by\n243\t  cases k_1\n244\t  cases k_2\n245\t  congr 1\n246\t\n247\t/-! ## §5 Substantive baseline witness\n248\t\n249\tConstant functions trivially satisfy `IsMazurMonotone` (with the\n250\tzero derivative everywhere), and identical Ernst potentials trivially\n251\tsatisfy `IsErnstPotentialPair`. These witnesses confirm the predicates\n252\tare non-vacuously inhabitable.\n253\t-/\n254\t\n255\t/--\n256\t**Substantive baseline witness:** the constant function `fun _ => c`\n257\tsatisfies `IsMazurMonotone` on `[0, L]` (for `0 ≤ L`) with the zero\n258\tderivative everywhere. {"parentUuid":"3773688e-e447-47eb-b63f-6b036ac36d9d","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"d7b89794-41f0-46fe-9a20-6d198f8f624f","agentId":"a1b16a56a1a83bc81","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01XsgNn2Kpah18Hr7qtGjLUA","type":"tool_result","content":"240\tuses linearity of `scalarOf` (`Ric = 0 ⟹ R = 0 ⟹ G = 0`).\n241\t-/\n242\t\n243\t/-- **Vacuum-Einstein characterization**: `G_{μν} = 0 ↔ Ric_{μν} = 0`\n244\tin 4D, with `R_scalar = scalarOf Ric g_inv`.\n245\t\n246\tForward: `G = 0` ⟹ trace `−R = 0` ⟹ `R = 0` ⟹ `Ric = G + (1/2) · R\n247\t· g = 0`. Backward: `Ric = 0` ⟹ `R = scalarOf 0 g_inv = 0` ⟹ `G = 0`.\n248\tThe hypothesis `(g_inv : g) = 4` is load-bearing for the forward\n249\ttrace step. -/\n250\ttheorem einsteinTensor_zero_iff_ricci_zero\n251\t    {Ric : RicciTensor} (g g_inv : MetricMatrix)\n252\t    (h_dim : sumFin4 (fun μ => sumFin4 (fun ν => g_inv μ ν * g μ ν))\n253\t             = 4) :\n254\t    (∀ μ ν, einsteinTensor Ric (scalarOf Ric g_inv) g μ ν = 0)\n255\t    ↔ (∀ μ ν, Ric μ ν = 0) := by\n256\t  constructor\n257\t  · -- Forward: G = 0 ⟹ Ric = 0\n258\t    intro hG\n259\t    -- Take the trace: scalarOf G g_inv = -R, but G = 0 gives scalarOf G g_inv = 0\n260\t    have hTrace := einsteinTensor_trace_eq_neg_scalar Ric g g_inv h_dim\n261\t    -- scalarOf (fun μ ν => 0) g_inv = 0:\n262\t    have hG_fun : (fun μ ν => einsteinTensor Ric (scalarOf Ric g_inv) g μ ν)\n263\t                  = (fun _ _ => (0 : ℝ)) := by\n264\t      funext μ ν; exact hG μ ν\n265\t    have hG_scalar : scalarOf\n266\t        (fun μ ν => einsteinTensor Ric (scalarOf Ric g_inv) g μ ν)\n267\t        g_inv = 0 := by\n268\t      rw [hG_fun]; unfold scalarOf sumFin4; ring\n269\t    -- Combining: -scalarOf Ric g_inv = 0, so scalarOf Ric g_inv = 0\n270\t    have hR_eq : scalarOf Ric g_inv = 0 := by linarith [hG_scalar.symm.trans hTrace]\n271\t    -- Now Ric μ ν = G μ ν + (1/2) R g μ ν = 0 + 0 = 0\n272\t    intro μ ν\n273\t    have := hG μ ν\n274\t    unfold einsteinTensor at this\n275\t    rw [hR_eq] at this\n276\t    linarith\n277\t  · -- Backward: Ric = 0 ⟹ G = 0\n278\t    intro hR μ ν\n279\t    unfold einsteinTensor\n280\t    have hR_scalar : scalarOf Ric g_inv = 0 := by\n281\t      have hPt : scalarOf Ric g_inv =\n282\t          scalarOf (fun _ _ => (0 : ℝ)) g_inv :=\n283\t        scalarOf_eq_of_pointwise g_inv hR\n284\t      rw [hPt]\n285\t      unfold scalarOf sumFin4\n286\t      ring\n287\t    rw [hR_scalar, hR μ ν]\n288\t    ring\n289\t\n290\t/-! ## §7 — De Sitter Λ-vacuum cross-bridge\n291\t\n292\t`G_{μν} + Λ · g_{μν} = 0 ↔ Λ = 3K` for the constant-K witness in 4D.\n293\t\n294\tAlgebraic-level cross-bridge to Phase 6c W1's Zhitnitsky-DE program.\n295\tThe relation `Λ = 3K` is the Einstein-equation form of de Sitter\n296\t`R^ρ_{σμν} = K(g_{σν} δ^ρ_μ − g_{σμ} δ^ρ_ν)`; in physics notation,\n297\t`H² = K = Λ/3` with `H` the de Sitter Hubble parameter.\n298\t\n299\tWe show, for the constant-K Riemann witness with non-degenerate\n300\tmetric (encoded as a non-zero metric at some sample index), that the\n301\tΛ-vacuum equation forces `Λ = 3K`. The substantive content is:\n302\tcombining the constant-K Einstein-tensor formula `G = −3K g` with\n303\tthe Λ-vacuum equation `G + Λ g = 0` gives `(Λ − 3K) g = 0`; if `g`\n304\tis non-zero anywhere then `Λ = 3K`.\n305\t-/\n306\t\n307\t/-- **De Sitter Λ-vacuum cross-bridge**: for the constant-K Riemann\n308\twitness, `G_{μν} + Λ · g_{μν} = 0` (at every index pair where `g`\n309\titself is non-zero) **iff** `Λ = 3K`.\n310\t\n311\tThe forward direction needs ONE non-zero metric component as\n312\tnon-degeneracy witness; we phrase it via existence-of-a-non-zero-`g`\n313\thypothesis to keep the statement self-contained. -/\n314\ttheorem constantSectional_lambda_vacuum_iff\n315\t    (K Λ : ℝ) (g g_inv : MetricMatrix)\n316\t    (h_dim : sumFin4 (fun μ => sumFin4 (fun ν => g_inv μ ν * g μ ν))\n317\t             = 4)\n318\t    (μ₀ ν₀ : Fin 4) (h_nondeg : g μ₀ ν₀ ≠ 0) :\n319\t    (∀ μ ν,\n320\t      einsteinTensor (ricciOf (constantSectionalRiemann K g))\n321\t                     (scalarOf (ricciOf (constantSectionalRiemann K g))\n322\t                               g_inv)\n323\t                     g μ ν\n324\t        + Λ * g μ ν = 0)\n325\t    ↔ Λ = 3 * K := by\n326\t  constructor\n327\t  · intro h\n328\t    have h0 := h μ₀ ν₀\n329\t    rw [constantSectional_einsteinTensor_eq K g g_inv h_dim μ₀ ν₀] at h0"}]},"uuid":"de51c375-d370-4ff1-bdc0-32a83d1fe102","timestamp":"2026-08-15T19:44:39.637Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"3773688e-e447-47eb-b63f-6b036ac36d9d","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/johnroehm/Programming/PythonEnvironments/Physics/Fluid-Based-Physics-Research/SK_EFT_Hawking","sessionId":"6cf37aa2-f1c7-4e33-a712-f17c2d75d99b","version":"2.1.229","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"swift-singing-llama"}
{"parentUuid":"29dd9ad3-ab0a-4dd4-b233-0d0928438b4c","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"d7b89794-41f0-46fe-9a20-6d198f8f624f","agentId":"a1b16a56a1a83bc81","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_0139QybiphUi4syWoofUe1VN","type":"tool_result","content":"96\t`MetricMatrix` for downstream interoperability. -/\n97\tabbrev EinsteinTensorType : Type := Fin 4 → Fin 4 → ℝ\n98\t\n99\t/-! ## §2 — Definition -/\n100\t\n101\t/-- **Einstein tensor**: `G_{μν} := Ric_{μν} − (1/2) R g_{μν}`.\n102\t\n103\tFree parameter `R_scalar : ℝ` represents the scalar curvature.\n104\tPhysical content arises by setting `R_scalar = scalarOf Ric g_inv`\n105\t(see trace identity in §4). -/\n106\tnoncomputable def einsteinTensor (Ric : RicciTensor) (R_scalar : ℝ)\n107\t    (g : MetricMatrix) : EinsteinTensorType :=\n108\t  fun μ ν => Ric μ ν - (1/2) * R_scalar * g μ ν\n109\t\n110\t/-! ## §3 — Symmetry\n111\t\n112\tSubstantive: hypothesis is `Ric` symmetric ∧ metric symmetric. Both\n113\tare load-bearing — dropping either yields a non-symmetric `G`.\n114\t-/\n115\t\n116\t/-- **Symmetry**: `G_{μν} = G_{νμ}` whenever Ricci and metric are\n117\tboth symmetric. Both hypotheses are load-bearing: the `(1/2) R g`\n118\tsubtraction inherits each tensor's antisymmetric part. -/\n119\ttheorem einsteinTensor_symm\n120\t    {Ric : RicciTensor} {R_scalar : ℝ} {g : MetricMatrix}\n121\t    (h_ric : ∀ μ ν, Ric μ ν = Ric ν μ)\n122\t    (h_g : MetricSymmetric g)\n123\t    (μ ν : Fin 4) :\n124\t    einsteinTensor Ric R_scalar g μ ν =\n125\t      einsteinTensor Ric R_scalar g ν μ := by\n126\t  unfold einsteinTensor\n127\t  rw [h_ric μ ν, h_g μ ν]\n128\t\n129\t/-! ## §4 — Linearity-of-trace + 4D trace identity\n130\t\n131\tThe dimension-4 trace identity `G^μ_μ = −R` decomposes into\n132\t\n133\t  (i) **algebraic linearity**: `scalarOf G g_inv = scalarOf Ric g_inv −\n134\t      (1/2) R · (g_inv : g)` for any dimension `n` (where the\n135\t      contraction `(g_inv : g) := Σ_{μν} g^{μν} g_{μν}` equals `n`);\n136\t  (ii) **dimension specialization**: for `n = 4` and\n137\t       `R = scalarOf Ric g_inv`, the linearity collapses to `−R`.\n138\t\n139\tWe ship (i) as a P3-trivial-class lemma (docstring-flagged) and (ii)\n140\tas the substantive quantitative-content theorem.\n141\t-/\n142\t\n143\t/-- **Linearity of `scalarOf` over the Einstein-tensor decomposition.**\n144\t\n145\tP3-trivial-class plumbing (`unfold` + `ring`); the dimension factor\n146\tappears in the `(g_inv : g)` sum, not here. We separate the algebraic\n147\tlinearity from the dimension specialization to keep both small. -/\n148\ttheorem einsteinTensor_scalar_linearity\n149\t    (Ric : RicciTensor) (R_scalar : ℝ) (g g_inv : MetricMatrix) :\n150\t    scalarOf (einsteinTensor Ric R_scalar g) g_inv\n151\t      = scalarOf Ric g_inv -\n152\t        (1/2) * R_scalar *\n153\t          sumFin4 (fun μ => sumFin4 (fun ν => g_inv μ ν * g μ ν)) := by\n154\t  unfold scalarOf einsteinTensor sumFin4\n155\t  ring\n156\t\n157\t/-- **Dimension-4 trace identity** `G^μ_μ = −R`.\n158\t\n159\tSubstantive quantitative-content theorem: the dimension factor `n − 2\n160\t= 2` for `n = 4` emerges algebraically — the trace of the\n161\t`(1/2) R g_{μν}` subtraction equals `(1/2) R · n`, so the trace of\n162\t`G_{μν} = Ric_{μν} − (1/2) R g_{μν}` equals `R − (n/2) R = −R` only at\n163\t`n = 4`. -/\n164\ttheorem einsteinTensor_trace_eq_neg_scalar\n165\t    (Ric : RicciTensor) (g g_inv : MetricMatrix)"}]},"uuid":"4ee23a8e-3e50-400c-9f83-b881fe429603","timestamp":"2026-08-15T19:44:39.638Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"29dd9ad3-ab0a-4dd4-b233-0d0928438b4c","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/johnroehm/Programming/PythonEnvironments/Physics/Fluid-Based-Physics-Research/SK_EFT_Hawking","sessionId":"6cf37aa2-f1c7-4e33-a712-f17c2d75d99b","version":"2.1.229","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"swift-singing-llama"}

{"parentUuid":"09636116-0ac2-459b-8080-80ba56688956","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a1b16a56a1a83bc81","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011Ce57ChUFQTr5YQegRK3z8","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Below is the section and appendix. Report follows the LaTeX.\n\n```latex\n\\section{What the formal substrate carries independently of the calibration}\n\\label{sec:bh-theorems}\n\nNothing in this section spends the heat-kernel coefficient. Every calibrated\nstatement so far has drawn on the same single piece of short-distance data, and a\nreader arriving here after seven sections of that accounting is entitled to ask\nwhere the budget has gone. It has not been touched. The singularity, area and\nhorizon-uniqueness content below is classical geometry formalised on its own\nterms, and it stands whatever value the calibration takes. It earns its space\nbecause it is the geometric ground on which the preceding thermodynamic\nstatements are read: an area law presupposes a horizon whose area is monotone, a\ntemperature is assigned to a horizon whose causal structure is fixed, and a\nmicroscopic entropy is counted on a surface whose existence is a geometric\nhypothesis. Machine-checking that layer, and stating exactly how far the machine\nchecking reaches, is what keeps the thermodynamic claims off an informal picture.\n\nThe scope caveat matters as much as the content, so we give it once and plainly.\nWhat is formalised is a predicate layer. Causal relations, energy conditions,\nnull-expansion scalars and horizon areas are carried as propositions and as\nreal-valued functions of real parameters, and the theorems relate those\npropositions to one another and discharge the real-analysis facts on which the\nclassical arguments turn. Curves, geodesics and affine completeness are not\nmodelled. No statement in this section is a machine-checked proof of a classical\nsingularity or uniqueness theorem, and at each point we say which part is a\nderivation and which is a rearrangement of definitions.\n\n\\subsection{The singularity content and its exact reach}\n\\label{sec:bh-theorems-penrose}\n\nThe substrate declares a trapped surface directly on expansion data:\n\\texttt{PenroseSingularity.IsTrappedSurface} holds of a non-empty set of events\ntogether with an outgoing and an ingoing null-expansion field when both\nexpansion scalars are strictly negative at every point of the set. The\nsingularity hypothesis \\texttt{PenroseSingularity.IsPenroseHypothesisSatisfied}\nis the conjunction of the null energy condition on a stress-energy bilinear form,\nthe trapped-surface predicate, and global hyperbolicity of the ambient causal\nstructure. The applicability predicate\n\\texttt{PenroseSingularity.IsADWPenroseApplicable} is the purely geometric part\nof that conjunction: trapped surface together with global hyperbolicity, with no\ncondition on the matter content.\n\nThe headline statement,\n\\texttt{PenroseSingularity.correctness\\_push\\_biconditional\\_under\\_applicable},\nrelates exactly these. Given the applicability predicate, the singularity\nhypothesis holds if and only if the null energy condition holds for the\nstress-energy form. Two things about this must be said in the open. First, the\nright-hand side of the biconditional is the null energy condition and not\ngeodesic incompleteness; the theorem does not assert that anything is singular.\nSecond, because the applicability predicate is precisely the non-energy part of\nthe hypothesis, the equivalence is a projection of a conjunction onto its\nremaining conjunct and a reconstruction of the conjunction from it. It is a\ndefinitional unfolding relative to the applicability predicate, not a derivation.\nIts worth is bookkeeping worth: it certifies that once the emergent geometry is\nfixed, the energy condition on the emergent stress-energy is the single remaining\ndiscriminator, so a later determination of that one predicate settles the\nhypothesis outright.\n\nThe mathematical content of the focusing argument sits elsewhere, and it is a\ngenuine derivation. The substrate carries the closed-form Riccati solution\n$\\theta(\\lambda) = \\theta_0 / (1 + \\theta_0 \\lambda / 3)$ of\n$\\mathrm{d}\\theta/\\mathrm{d}\\lambda = -\\theta^2/3$ with $\\theta(0) = \\theta_0$,\nwhich is the four-dimensional shear-free, curvature-suppressed form of the\nfocusing equation for the expansion of a null congruence. For $\\theta_0 < 0$ the\ndenominator is strictly positive on $[0, -3/\\theta_0)$, so the solution stays\nstrictly negative on that interval\n(\\texttt{PenroseSingularity.riccatiSolution\\_neg}), and the focal parameter\n$-3/\\theta_0$ is itself strictly positive\n(\\texttt{PenroseSingularity.focusingTime\\_pos}). The statement that consumes the\nfull trapped-surface hypothesis rather than half of it is\n\\texttt{PenroseSingularity.penrose\\_both\\_focal\\_times\\_pos}: at any point of a\ntrapped surface, both the outgoing and the ingoing focal parameters are strictly\npositive, so both null normal congruences focus at finite affine parameter. The\nstep from a finite focal parameter to failure of geodesic extension is the\ncurve-theoretic step, and it is not present. The conclusion predicate\n\\texttt{PenroseSingularity.PenroseConclusion} is defined, and the incompleteness\npredicate it uses is carried as a positivity condition on an affine-parameter\nbound rather than as a statement about a geodesic on a manifold; no theorem\nderives that conclusion from the hypothesis. The classical statement associated\nwith Penrose~\\cite{Penrose1965} is therefore not proved here, and we do not claim\nit.\n\nOne non-degeneracy check is worth recording, because a predicate layer of this\nkind can be made vacuous by accident. On the concrete one-dimensional causal\norder used as the substrate's witness spacetime, a trapped surface cannot exist\nonce the expansion fields are non-negative\n(\\texttt{PenroseSingularity.realLineSpacetime\\_no\\_trappedSurface\\_for\\_nonneg\\_expansion}),\nso the trapped-surface predicate genuinely distinguishes the witness from a\nfour-dimensional geometry rather than holding everywhere.\n\n\\subsection{The strong-energy-condition variant is unavailable, and this follows\nfrom the same number}\n\\label{sec:bh-theorems-hp}\n\nThe strong-energy variant associated with Hawking and\nPenrose~\\cite{HawkingPenrose1970} is formalised the same way, with the strong\nenergy condition replacing the null one:\n\\texttt{HawkingPenroseSingularity.IsHawkingPenroseHypothesisSatisfied} conjoins\nthe strong energy condition, the trapped-surface predicate and global\nhyperbolicity, and\n\\texttt{HawkingPenroseSingularity.hawkingPenrose\\_biconditional\\_under\\_applicable}\nreduces it, under the same geometric applicability predicate, to the strong\nenergy condition alone. That reduction is the same definitional rearrangement as\nabove.\n\nWhat is not a rearrangement is the obstruction. For a stress-energy form\n$T_{\\mu\\nu} = -\\Lambda g_{\\mu\\nu}$ on the flat metric, the strong-energy\ncombination evaluated on the explicit future-directed timelike witness\n$v = (1,0,0,0)$, with the trace parameter carried as $-4\\Lambda$, comes out equal\nto $\\Lambda\\, g(v,v)$, which is strictly negative for every positive $\\Lambda$.\nThat is \\texttt{EnergyConditions.cosmologicalLambda\\_violates\\_SEC}, discharged\non an explicit witness rather than an existential. Feeding it into the hypothesis\ngives \\texttt{HawkingPenroseSingularity.cosmologicalLambda\\_violates\\_HP\\_hypothesis}:\nfor every positive $\\Lambda$, the strong-energy hypothesis fails outright on that\nstress-energy.\n\nThis is where the section touches the spine of the paper. The emergent\ncosmological constant of this framework is strictly positive, and that positivity\nis itself machine-checked\n(\\texttt{MicroscopicCoefficientMatch.lambdaEmergMicroscopic\\_pos}). It is the same\nconstant whose magnitude is reported in \\S\\ref{sec:tests}, where its overshoot\nagainst the observed value is the framework's sharpest quantitative liability.\nHere the same number has a second consequence, and a purely structural one:\nbecause the constant is positive, the substrate's own cosmological sector\nviolates the strong energy condition, and the strong-energy route to singularity\nformation is therefore closed to it. Composing the two verified facts requires\nnothing further, since the violation theorem is quantified over every positive\n$\\Lambda$.\n\nWe state the consequence as a result rather than as an omission. Singularity\nformation in this framework has to be argued on the null energy condition alone,\nby the route of \\S\\ref{sec:bh-theorems-penrose}; the stronger hypothesis is not\nmerely unproven here but unavailable in principle to a sector with a positive\ncosmological constant. A referee weighing the cosmological-constant overshoot\nshould weigh this alongside it: one number carries both the quantitative cost and\nthis structural restriction, and both are consequences of the same positivity.\n\n\\subsection{The area theorem on the Schwarzschild family}\n\\label{sec:bh-theorems-area}\n\nOn the Schwarzschild family the horizon area is $A(M) = 16\\pi M^2$, and its\nmonotonicity in the mass is discharged algebraically. For a mass history\n$M : \\mathbb{R} \\to \\mathbb{R}$ that is non-negative and non-decreasing, the\ncomposed area history $t \\mapsto A(M(t))$ is non-decreasing\n(\\texttt{AreaTheorem.schwarzschild\\_horizon\\_area\\_nonDecreasing}), and on\nstrictly positive masses the dependence is strict: $0 < M_1 < M_2$ implies\n$A(M_1) < A(M_2)$ (\\texttt{AreaTheorem.schwarzschild\\_area\\_strictly\\_increasing}).\nThe non-decreasing form is what the second law needs; the strict form is what\nrules out an area that stalls under genuine accretion. Neither assumes anything\nabout the calibration, and both are elementary consequences of the closed form\ntogether with monotonicity of squaring on the non-negative reals.\n\nA second, curve-level statement carries the evolution content rather than the\nclosed form. Along a null generator parameterised by an affine parameter on a\ncompact interval, suppose the area obeys $A'(\\lambda) = \\theta(\\lambda)\nA(\\lambda)$, is non-negative and continuous, and the expansion satisfies\n$\\theta(\\lambda) \\ge 0$ on the interior. Then the area is monotone\nnon-decreasing on the interval\n(\\texttt{AreaTheoremCurveTheoretic.horizon\\_area\\_curve\\_monotone}). This is the\none-dimensional real-analysis core of the classical argument of\nHawking~\\cite{Hawking1971}, and it is a derivation. The non-negativity of the\nexpansion is a hypothesis of the statement, encoding the reduction that the\nclassical argument performs from the null energy condition together with cosmic\ncensorship. That reduction is not itself formalised, so the theorem should be\nread as: given non-negative expansion on the horizon generators, monotone area\nfollows rigorously.\n\nThe bridge to the thermodynamic reading is a positive rescaling. In units with\n$G_N = 1$, a non-decreasing area function yields a non-decreasing\nBekenstein--Hawking entropy $S(t) = A(t)/4$\n(\\texttt{AreaTheorem.area\\_theorem\\_implies\\_bh\\_entropy\\_monotone}). This gives\nthe geometric route to the second law of black-hole\nmechanics~\\cite{BardeenCarterHawking1973} independently of the dissipative\nentropy-current argument and of the microscopic counting used earlier in this\npaper, so the three routes converge on the same substrate without sharing an\ninput.\n\n\\subsection{The Kerr family and the sub-extremality bound}\n\\label{sec:bh-theorems-nohair}\n\nThe stationary family is carried as a parameter record\n\\texttt{NoHairTheorem.KerrFamilyParams} holding a mass $M$ and a signed angular\nmomentum $J$, with strict mass positivity and the sub-extremality condition\n$J^2 \\le M^4$ imposed as structural invariants rather than as later hypotheses.\nAny inhabitant of that record therefore satisfies the bound by construction, and\nthe equivalent absolute-value form $|J| \\le M^2$ is derived from it by a\nsquare-root comparison (\\texttt{NoHairTheorem.subextremality\\_implies\\_abs\\_J\\_le\\_M\\_sq}).\nThis is where extremality enters the formal layer: parameters beyond the bound\nare not merely undesirable, they cannot be constructed.\n\nThe $J = 0$ specialisation is discharged against the exact-solution machinery\nrather than asserted. The zero-angular-momentum member has horizon radius\n$r_+ = 2M$ (\\texttt{NoHairTheorem.kerr\\_family\\_schwarzschild\\_horizon\\_radius})\nand horizon area $16\\pi M^2$\n(\\texttt{NoHairTheorem.kerr\\_family\\_schwarzschild\\_area}), matching the area used\nin \\S\\ref{sec:bh-theorems-area}, and inherits the area monotonicity in the mass\n(\\texttt{NoHairTheorem.kerr\\_family\\_schwarzschild\\_area\\_monotone}). These are\ngenuine cross-checks: they confirm that the parameterised family and the\nindependently defined Schwarzschild quantities agree where they overlap.\n\nThe statement named \\texttt{NoHairTheorem.noHairTheorem\\_statement} requires care,\nbecause its name promises more than its type delivers, and we would rather say so\nthan let a reader infer otherwise. Its antecedent predicate\n\\texttt{NoHairTheorem.IsStationaryAxisymmetricVacuumBH} is declared at this layer\nwithout differential-geometric content: stationarity, axisymmetry, the vacuum\nfield equations and the presence of a horizon are named in its documentation but\nare not modelled in its definition. Its conclusion,\n\\texttt{NoHairTheorem.HasKerrFamilyDescription}, asserts the existence of a\nparameter record of positive mass and does not refer to the spacetime supplied to\nit. The theorem, in consequence, fixes the interface between the two predicates\nand constructs the witness through the $J = 0$ member; it establishes nothing\nabout uniqueness. Nothing in this paper should be read as a machine-checked proof\nof black-hole uniqueness in the sense of Israel~\\cite{Israel1967},\nCarter~\\cite{Carter1971}, Hawking~\\cite{Hawking1972} or\nRobinson~\\cite{Robinson1975}.\n\nUniqueness content that is machine-checked exists, at a reduced scope, and is\nworth separating from the above. The rigidity mechanism of the $\\sigma$-model\nargument distils to a real-analysis statement: a function that is continuous on a\ncompact interval, differentiable on its interior with non-negative derivative,\nand takes equal values at the two endpoints is constant on the interval\n(\\texttt{MazurSigmaModelRigidity.mazur\\_monotone\\_rigidity}). Applying it to the\nsquared difference of a pair of Ernst potentials with matching boundary data\nforces the two potentials to coincide throughout\n(\\texttt{MazurSigmaModelRigidity.ernst\\_potential\\_coincidence}). That is a\nderivation, and it is the shape of the classical rigidity argument with the\nelliptic and $\\sigma$-model geometry replaced by its one-dimensional consequence;\nthe non-negativity of the derivative, which the classical argument obtains from\nthe divergence identity, is a hypothesis here. At the parameter level, two\nrecords with equal mass and equal angular momentum are equal\n(\\texttt{MazurSigmaModelRigidity.kerrFamilyParams\\_unique\\_from\\_M\\_J}); this is\nrecord extensionality over the two real fields, and it certifies that the\nparameterisation carries no hidden data, not that the family is exhaustive.\n\n\\subsection{What this layer licenses}\n\nTaken together, the results of this section license three readings and refuse a\nfourth. They license the claim that the geometric hypotheses under which the\npreceding thermodynamics is stated are written down precisely and are mutually\nconsistent, since the predicates are inhabited by an explicit witness and are not\nsatisfied vacuously. They license the focusing and monotonicity facts as\ntheorems, because those are real-analysis derivations with no gap. They license\nthe structural claim of \\S\\ref{sec:bh-theorems-hp}, which is a consequence of a\nverified positivity and a verified counterexample. They do not license any claim\nthat the classical singularity, area or uniqueness theorems have been reproved by\nmachine; the curve-theoretic and elliptic steps that carry those theorems are\nabsent, and the predicate layer is honest scaffolding for them rather than a\nsubstitute.\n\n%% =====================================================================\n%% APPENDIX\n%% =====================================================================\n\n\\section{The algebraic Lorentzian-geometry substrate}\n\\label{app:lorentzian-substrate}\n\nThis appendix records the geometric layer that\n\\S\\ref{sec:bh-theorems} consumes. It is written to be consulted rather than read\nthrough, and it is needed because general-purpose formalised-mathematics\nlibraries currently supply Riemannian, that is positive-definite, metric\nstructure, together with a covariant-derivative and torsion interface, but no\nindefinite-signature counterpart, no curvature objects built on that interface,\nand no causal structure. Each item below states what is defined, what is proved,\nand where the boundary of the proof lies.\n\n\\paragraph{Indefinite-signature metric structure.}\nAt the matrix level, \\texttt{LorentzianMetric.IsLorentzianSignature} asserts of a\n$4 \\times 4$ real array that the time-time entry is $-1$, the remaining diagonal\nentries are $+1$, and the off-diagonal entries vanish;\n\\texttt{LorentzianMetric.IsLorentzianMetric} adds symmetry and non-degeneracy, and\nthe Minkowski array is verified to satisfy it. The separation from the\npositive-definite case is proved rather than asserted: a positive-definite form\ncannot satisfy the Lorentzian signature predicate\n(\\texttt{LorentzianMetric.riemannian\\_not\\_lorentzian\\_signature}). At the bundle\nlevel, \\texttt{LorentzianBundle.IsLorentzianBundle} asks of a family of fibrewise\nbilinear forms on the tangent spaces only that each be symmetric and\nnon-degenerate. Positive-definiteness is nowhere assumed, which is the point:\nthe constructions below are semi-Riemannian and apply to indefinite signature\nwithout modification. Non-degeneracy yields injectivity of the pairing in either\nslot (\\texttt{LorentzianBundle.lorentzianBundle\\_pairing\\_left\\_injective} and\n\\texttt{LorentzianBundle.lorentzianBundle\\_pairing\\_right\\_injective}).\n\n\\paragraph{Levi-Civita uniqueness by the Koszul formula.}\nAt the matrix level the Koszul closed form for the Christoffel symbols is\ndefined with the metric partial derivatives supplied as explicit inputs rather\nthan computed, which makes the resulting identities independent of any coordinate\napparatus, and it is proved symmetric in its lower indices\n(\\texttt{RiemannianConnection.christoffelKoszul\\_isTorsionFree}). At the bundle\nlevel the substantive uniqueness argument is carried in full. The predicate\n\\texttt{LeviCivita.IsLeviCivita} conjoins the covariant-derivative interface,\ntorsion-freeness, symmetry of the fibre metric, and metric compatibility\nexpressed through the exterior derivative of the scalar pairing. From it the\nKoszul identity is derived\n(\\texttt{LeviCivita.koszul\\_identity\\_of\\_isLeviCivita}), and two connections\nsatisfying the predicate for the same metric are shown to agree pointwise\n(\\texttt{LeviCivita.leviCivita\\_unique\\_of\\_isLeviCivita}). The specialisation to\nthe indefinite-signature case is\n\\texttt{LorentzianBundle.leviCivita\\_unique\\_for\\_lorentzianBundle}, which supplies\nthe non-degeneracy input from the symmetric non-degenerate fibre metric directly.\n\n\\paragraph{The algebraic first Bianchi identity.}\nFor a covariant derivative on the tangent bundle that is torsion-free in the\nsense that the antisymmetrised derivative of two sections equals their Lie\nbracket, the cyclic sum of the curvature operator vanishes:\n$R(X,Y)Z + R(Y,Z)X + R(Z,X)Y = 0$\n(\\texttt{BundleRiemann.bundleRiemannAux\\_first\\_bianchi\\_torsionfree\\_apply}, with\nthe variant \\texttt{bundleRiemannAux\\_first\\_bianchi\\_torsionfree\\_of\\_isCovariantDerivativeOn}\nthat consumes the library's covariant-derivative interface in place of an\nabstract additivity hypothesis). The proof runs through a pointwise cyclic Jacobi\nidentity for the manifold Lie bracket\n(\\texttt{BundleRiemann.mlieBracket\\_cyclic\\_jacobi\\_apply}), which is derived from\nthe library's Leibniz form together with antisymmetry and pointwise negation\ndistribution. Two scope points: no metric enters this statement, so it is\nsignature-agnostic and holds for indefinite signature as a special case; and the\nidentity is the algebraic one, the differential second Bianchi identity being\nsupported only through antisymmetry machinery elsewhere.\n\n\\paragraph{Algebraic Riemann and Einstein tensors.}\nThe Christoffel-quadratic part of the Riemann tensor is defined and proved\nantisymmetric in its last index pair\n(\\texttt{RiemannianConnection.riemannQuadraticTerm\\_AntisymLastTwo}); the part\ninvolving derivatives of the Christoffel symbols is outside this scope. The\nEinstein tensor is defined as $G_{\\mu\\nu} = R_{\\mu\\nu} - \\tfrac{1}{2} R\ng_{\\mu\\nu}$, is symmetric when the Ricci tensor and the metric are\n(\\texttt{EinsteinTensor.einsteinTensor\\_symm}), and satisfies the\ndimension-dependent trace identity $G^{\\mu}{}_{\\mu} = -R$, where the dimension\nenters through the contraction of the metric with its inverse\n(\\texttt{EinsteinTensor.einsteinTensor\\_trace\\_eq\\_neg\\_scalar}). The vacuum\ncharacterisation $G_{\\mu\\nu} = 0 \\Leftrightarrow R_{\\mu\\nu} = 0$ is a\nbiconditional with the dimension contraction as a load-bearing hypothesis\n(\\texttt{EinsteinTensor.einsteinTensor\\_zero\\_iff\\_ricci\\_zero}), and for the\nconstant-sectional-curvature witness the $\\Lambda$-vacuum equation is equivalent\nto $\\Lambda = 3K$ (\\texttt{EinsteinTensor.constantSectional\\_lambda\\_vacuum\\_iff}).\n\n\\paragraph{Energy conditions.}\nThe null, weak, dominant and strong energy conditions are predicates on an\nabstract stress-energy bilinear form paired with a metric, in signature\n$(-,+,+,+)$, with the timelike and null restrictions load-bearing in each case\nand the trace supplied externally for the strong condition. The chain from\ndominant to weak is a projection\n(\\texttt{EnergyConditions.DEC\\_implies\\_WEC}); the step from weak to null carries\nits approximation hypothesis explicitly, as a sequence of future-directed\ntimelike vectors along which the stress-energy converges\n(\\texttt{EnergyConditions.WEC\\_implies\\_NEC\\_under\\_continuity}). Non-implications\nare witnessed by explicit tensors and explicit vectors rather than by\nexistentials: the cosmological-constant form satisfies the weak and null\nconditions and violates the strong one\n(\\texttt{EnergyConditions.cosmologicalLambda\\_WEC},\n\\texttt{EnergyConditions.cosmologicalLambda\\_NEC},\n\\texttt{EnergyConditions.cosmologicalLambda\\_violates\\_SEC}); a ghost scalar with\nspacelike gradient violates the null condition\n(\\texttt{EnergyConditions.ghostScalar\\_violates\\_NEC}); and a perfect fluid with\npressure exceeding density violates the dominant condition on a named pair of\nfuture-directed timelike vectors\n(\\texttt{EnergyConditions.stiff\\_fluid\\_violates\\_DEC}).\n\n\\paragraph{Tetrad and metric.}\nThe tetrad-induced metric $g_{\\mu\\nu} = \\eta_{ab} e^a{}_\\mu e^b{}_\\nu$ is defined\nby explicit contraction and proved symmetric for every tetrad configuration\n(\\texttt{TetradFormalism.tetradInducedMetric\\_symm}), and the canonical frame\n$e^a{}_\\mu = \\delta^a{}_\\mu$ recovers the flat metric exactly\n(\\texttt{TetradFormalism.minkowskiTetrad\\_induces\\_minkowski\\_metric}). The\nreduction of the tetrad formulation to the metric formulation is tied to the\nvanishing of the torsion residual, which happens exactly at unit Einstein--Cartan\nparameter (\\texttt{TetradFormalism.tetrad\\_levi\\_civita\\_iff\\_alpha\\_unity}). The\nconverse direction, recovery of a tetrad from a metric up to local Lorentz\ntransformation, is not formalised.\n\n\\paragraph{Canonical constraints.}\nThe Hamiltonian and momentum constraints of the canonical\nformulation~\\cite{ADM1962} are carried as scalar relations among the spatial\nscalar curvature, the trace and squared norm of the extrinsic curvature, and the\nmatter density and current. In vacuum the Hamiltonian constraint is equivalent to\n$^{(3)}R + (\\mathrm{tr}\\,K)^2 = K_{ij}K^{ij}$\n(\\texttt{ADMFormalism.hamiltonianConstraint\\_vacuum\\_iff}), at a moment of time\nsymmetry it reduces to $^{(3)}R = 16\\pi\\rho$\n(\\texttt{ADMFormalism.hamiltonianConstraint\\_moment\\_of\\_time\\_symmetry\\_iff}), and\nin vacuum the momentum constraint is equivalent to vanishing of the trace-free\ndivergence (\\texttt{ADMFormalism.momentumConstraint\\_vacuum\\_iff}). The spatial\ncovariant divergence is an input to these statements rather than a computed\nquantity, so the constraints are verified as scalar identities in the curvature\ninvariants and not at tensorial scope. Flat space satisfies both\n(\\texttt{ADMFormalism.minkowski\\_satisfies\\_hamiltonianConstraint},\n\\texttt{ADMFormalism.minkowski\\_satisfies\\_momentumConstraint}).\n\n\\paragraph{Causal structure and its witness.}\nA spacetime is carried as a topological space of events with a causal and a\nchronological relation subject to reflexivity of the causal relation,\ntransitivity of both, containment of the chronological relation in the causal\none, and mixed transitivity in either order. Causal and chronological futures and\npasts are the set-valued projections of these relations. The hierarchy is\ndeclared as predicates: absence of closed timelike loops; antisymmetry of the\ncausal relation; causal convexity of small neighbourhoods; existence of a\ncontinuous time function strictly increasing along chronological pairs; and\nglobal hyperbolicity, defined as causal convexity together with compactness of\nevery causal diamond. Only one downward implication is proved, and it is proved\nhonestly: a strictly monotone continuous time function rules out closed timelike\nloops\n(\\texttt{CausalStructure.Spacetime.IsStablyCausal\\_implies\\_IsChronological}). The\nimplication from causal antisymmetry to absence of closed timelike loops is\nrecorded as not derivable from the relational axioms alone, because the textbook\nargument uses a notion of curve length that the relational layer cannot express,\nand it is deliberately not shipped. The witness is the real line ordered by\n$\\le$ and $<$, which is verified to have no closed timelike loops\n(\\texttt{CausalStructure.Spacetime.realLineSpacetime\\_isChronological}), to be\ncausally antisymmetric\n(\\texttt{CausalStructure.Spacetime.realLineSpacetime\\_isCausal}), to admit the\nidentity as a time function\n(\\texttt{CausalStructure.Spacetime.realLineSpacetime\\_isStablyCausal}), to have\ncausal and chronological futures equal to the closed and open upper half-lines,\nand to have causal diamonds equal to closed bounded intervals\n(\\texttt{CausalStructure.Spacetime.realLineSpacetime\\_causalDiamond\\_eq}), which\nsupplies the compactness input to global hyperbolicity by Heine--Borel. Every\nsingleton is a Cauchy surface for it\n(\\texttt{CausalStructure.Spacetime.realLineSpacetime\\_singleton\\_isCauchySurface}).\n\n\\paragraph{Candidates for a general-purpose library.}\nSeveral items above are stated at library generality rather than at the\ngenerality this paper needs, and are natural candidates for contribution to a\ngeneral-purpose formalised-mathematics library such as Mathlib4. The\nsymmetric non-degenerate fibre-metric predicate and its pairing-injectivity\nconsequences are signature-agnostic and phrased over an arbitrary nontrivially\nnormed field. The pointwise cyclic Jacobi identity for the manifold Lie bracket\nand the pointwise negation-distribution lemmas it rests on fill an identified gap\nbeside the library's existing Leibniz form. The algebraic first Bianchi identity\nfor torsion-free covariant derivatives on the tangent bundle, and the\nKoszul-formula derivation and Levi-Civita uniqueness theorem for any\nnon-degenerate symmetric fibre metric, are stated over the same generality and\ndepend on the library's covariant-derivative interface rather than on anything\nspecific to this paper. The predicate layers that are specialised to four\ndimensions or to a fixed index set, in particular the matrix-level signature\npredicates, the energy conditions and the canonical constraints, are not offered\nin that form.\n```\n\n## Bibkeys used, and gaps\n\nUsed, all already present in `papers/D3/paper_draft.tex`: `Penrose1965`, `HawkingPenrose1970`, `Hawking1971`, `BardeenCarterHawking1973`, `Israel1967`, `Carter1971`, `Hawking1972`, `Robinson1975`, `ADM1962`.\n\n**Absent from the D3 bibliography and therefore not cited** (you may want them added): `Mazur1982`, `Bunting1983`, `HawkingEllis1973`, `KobayashiNomizu1963`, `ONeill1983`, `Geroch1970`, `BernalSanchez2003`, `MinguzziSanchez2008`, `SenovillaGarfinkle2015`. The Mazur/Bunting absence is the one that costs content: the Ernst-potential paragraph attributes the rigidity mechanism only descriptively because the originating papers have no bibkey here.\n\n## Contradictions between the brief and the sources\n\n1. **`IsADWPenroseApplicable` has two conjuncts, not four, and NEC is not one of them.** The brief inherits the current draft's §24.3 description (\"requires *(i)* an ADW-substrate-scale tetrad, *(ii)* a trapped-surface hypothesis, *(iii)* a global-hyperbolicity witness, *(iv)* NEC closure\"). The definition is `IsTrappedSurface Sigma_set θ_plus θ_minus ∧ S.IsGloballyHyperbolic`. There is no tetrad condition and no NEC condition. I wrote the definition as it stands.\n\n2. **The biconditional does not relate the hypothesis to geodesic incompleteness.** The current draft says it states \"the Penrose hypothesis is satisfied if and only if the spacetime is null-geodesically incomplete to the future of the trapped surface.\" The actual type is `IsPenroseHypothesisSatisfied S T g Σ θ₊ θ₋ ↔ NEC T g`. The right-hand side is the null energy condition. This is the single largest factual error in the section being replaced; the drafted text states the true relation.\n\n3. **That biconditional is a definitional unfolding, not a derivation.** Proof: `refine ⟨fun h_pen => h_pen.1, fun h_NEC => ⟨h_NEC, h_app.1, h_app.2⟩⟩`. Since `IsADWPenroseApplicable` is exactly the non-NEC conjuncts of `IsPenroseHypothesisSatisfied`, the equivalence is `And` projection plus reconstruction. The module docstring's own claim that it is \"NOT `Iff.rfl`\" is true only in the narrow sense that `h_app` is consumed; there is no mathematical step. I said so explicitly, per your instruction about definitional unfoldings.\n\n4. **`NoHairTheorem.noHairTheorem_statement` is thin enough that the manuscript's ordinary voice would overstate it — this is the answer to your direct question.** `IsStationaryAxisymmetricVacuumBH (_S : Spacetime) : Prop := True`. `HasKerrFamilyDescription (_S : Spacetime) : Prop := ∃ k : KerrFamilyParams, k.M > 0` (the spacetime argument is discarded). The theorem additionally takes `(h_witness : ∃ M : ℝ, 0 < M)`, which is unconditionally true, and closes by constructing the `J = 0` member. So the type reduces to \"True → (∃M>0) → (∃ Kerr record with positive mass)\". **It contains no no-hair content whatsoever.** The bundle metadata's characterization (\"the Kerr-family no-hair statement\") and your brief item 4 (\"the Kerr-family no-hair statement\") cannot be written in the manuscript's ordinary voice. I wrote the honest description and an explicit disclaimer that nothing here is a machine-checked uniqueness proof. **If the lead wants a \"no-hair\" claim in the abstract or elsewhere in D3, it needs to be withdrawn or rewritten to the sub-extremality bound plus the Ernst distillation.** Treat this as a blocker for any sibling section that banks on it.\n\n5. **The first Bianchi identity is not \"on the indefinite-signature bundle.\"** `bundleRiemannAux_first_bianchi_torsionfree_apply` is stated over an arbitrary `NontriviallyNormedField 𝕜` on `TangentSpace I`, with no metric anywhere in the statement or hypotheses. It is signature-agnostic. I wrote that, and noted indefinite signature is a special case. The brief's phrasing would have been a misdescription.\n\n6. **The causal hierarchy does not \"close constructively.\"** The current draft asserts `stable ⇒ strong ⇒ causality ⇒ chronology` closes constructively. `CausalStructure.lean` §5 states the opposite in two places: `IsCausal ⟹ IsChronological` \"is NOT a consequence of our six Wald §8.1 axioms alone\" and is deliberately not shipped; `IsStablyCausal ⟹ IsCausal` is also not shipped. The only proved implication is `IsStablyCausal_implies_IsChronological`. The appendix says this.\n\n7. **`realLineSpacetime` is not proved to satisfy every causal predicate.** The current draft says it \"satisfies all four causal-structure predicates trivially\" and cites `realLineSpacetime_no_trappedSurface_for_nonneg_expansion`, which is a different theorem in a different module and is about trapped surfaces, not causality. Shipped witnesses are chronological, causal, stably causal, the two future identities, the causal-diamond identity and the Cauchy-surface statement. There is no `realLineSpacetime_isStronglyCausal` or `_isGloballyHyperbolic`. I wrote only what exists and phrased the diamond result as \"supplies the compactness input\" rather than as global hyperbolicity.\n\n8. **Tetrad-metric \"equivalence\" is one-directional.** The current draft claims \"the tetrad is recoverable from the metric up to local Lorentz transformation.\" No such theorem exists in `TetradFormalism.lean`. I stated the induced-metric direction and said the converse is not formalised.\n\n9. **Levi-Civita uniqueness does not live where the current draft says.** The draft credits `RiemannianConnection.lean`; that module's own docstring says uniqueness is \"intentionally deferred.\" The real theorems are `LeviCivita.leviCivita_unique_of_isLeviCivita` and its indefinite-signature specialisation `LorentzianBundle.leviCivita_unique_for_lorentzianBundle`. Also note `IsLorentzianBundle` is symmetry plus non-degeneracy only; signature is *not* part of it (module docstring: \"the Levi-Civita connection exists and is unique on any non-degenerate symmetric bilinear form, regardless of signature\"). Any \"first formalisation on Lorentzian metrics\" phrasing keyed on signature is therefore keyed on the wrong property.\n\n10. **The ADM constraints are scalar, not tensorial.** The current draft calls them \"Lean biconditionals over the indefinite-signature metric bundle.\" They are `linarith`-level biconditionals on real scalars with the spatial covariant divergence passed in as a parameter. I said so.\n\n11. **The area theorem's expansion non-negativity is a hypothesis.** `IsHorizonAreaCurve.theta_nonneg` is a field of the hypothesis structure; the reduction from NEC plus cosmic censorship to `θ ≥ 0` is not formalised. I flagged this rather than letting \"the area theorem closes\" stand.\n\n12. **Priority claims omitted deliberately.** Every module in this set carries \"First formalization in any proof assistant,\" sourced to an internal audit of one pinned library commit. I cannot verify a negative existence claim across all proof assistants, and the D3 bibliography has no citable survey to support it, so I made no priority claim anywhere in the section or appendix.
